# Scoping pass: FSM1/cipher-box #635 — rotation primitives, sweep, name wave, grants

## What's already in place (merged #735 / #628 / #632) vs. what #635 must add

The three dependencies are all **CLOSED/merged**: #628 (net resolve/publish/liveness), #632 (sync core: op queue, rebase, `sync/pointer.rs`), #633 (grants ledger/contact/mailbox, merged as PR #735). So #635 is unblocked.

What exists to build on:
- `net/` publish pipeline: `publish` (register-first CAS), `resolve`, `retire`/`root_retire_ready`, `eol_republish`, liveness — the publish/retire substrate rotation rides.
- `gate/adoption.rs`: the six-stage gate, `adopt` / `adopt_deferred` / `PendingAdoption`, floor law.
- `grants/`: **semantic composition only** — `self_locate`, `enforce_committed_ledger`, `accept_share`, revocation `classify`, owner `cross_check`. `ledger.rs` and `mod.rs` say explicitly *"it mints nothing (grant creation needs the rotation primitives of #635)."*
- `sync/`: op queue, rebase, overlay, `pointer.rs`, tick, staleness.
- `crates/core/src/seal/`: every sealing primitive rotation needs already exists — `seal_grant_blob`, `seal_owner_blob`, `seal_ascent_link`, `seal_history_link`, `sign_grant_set`, `sign_structure`, `encode_write_body`, `seal_read_body`, `OverrideSeedPayload`. **Rotation is composition; it introduces no new crypto.**

What does **not** exist yet (all greenfield in #635):
- **No `rotation` module at all** — no rotate/sweep/wave files anywhere under `crates/engine/src`.
- **No grant-section record layout.** `GrantSection` is only a private struct in `crates/core/examples/kat_gen.rs`; there is **no public typed encode/decode**. The gate's `Candidate` takes `commitment`, `structures`, `ascent`, `envelope` as *separately hand-fed* fields — nothing parses a scope-root's grant section out of `envelope.unknown["grantSection"]`. The assembly/parse of a published scope root is unbuilt.
- **No grant creation, invites, or facade wiring.** `Grant`/`Revoke`/`Downgrade`/`CreateInviteLink`/`AcceptShare`/`RotateNow` all return `EngineError::Unimplemented`; `accept_share` is a free function never wired into the facade.
- **No direct-child-scope index**, no eager-set enumeration, no owner-seed-cache/sent-index durable persistence.
- `ReceivedShareStore` has **only `persist`** — no `load`/hydrate (the rehydrate constraint).

---

## 1. Deliverable table

| # | Deliverable | Size | Touches |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **Grant-section record layout** — typed assemble/parse of a scope-root envelope's grant section (commitment + grant blobs + owner blob + ascent/history links + write-body + structure sigs) into/out of `envelope.unknown["grantSection"]`; frozen + KAT'd | M | new `grants/section.rs` (+ maybe a core `seal` typed `GrantSection`), `gate/adoption.rs` |
| D2 | **Direct-child-scope index** — per-scope index of child scope roots; maintained by grant/dissolution/cross-scope-move under dest-first + observed-repair; enumeration substrate for D3/D4 | M | new `rotation/` module, `sync/op.rs`+`rebase.rs`, write-body |
| D3 | **rotateScope** (read rotation) — override-seed mint; eager-set law (owner cascade over every transitively-reachable descendant scope root, each fully rotated / grantee flat self-contained); re-wrap committed blobs verbatim; owner blob, history link, ascent link; structure-signed by writer pseudonym | L | new `rotation/rotate_scope.rs`, `net::publish`, `gate/floor.rs`, entropy |
| D4 | **sweep** — idempotent epoch-lag work-list; epoch-converged convergence (pre-grant); direct-child-scope index self-heal + flag; metadata-only re-seal; idle-cadence `Scheduler` job; CAS-safe concurrent sweepers | M/L | new `rotation/sweep.rs`, `seams/scheduler.rs`, `net::publish` |
| D5 | **rotateScopeWrite + name wave** — commitment re-sign; write override seed; background child-first name wave, root re-pointed last; three-channel re-point publish (scope pointer / mailbox / old-name tombstone); inventory swap (register-first + batch retire); root linger | L | new `rotation/rotate_write.rs`, `sync/pointer.rs`, `net::{publish,retire}`, `mailbox`, `api` |
| D6 | **Trigger table** — full-depth scope-exit detection; immediate-cut read revoke; write revoke/downgrade; discovered-link-expiry (ledger field, next owner session acts); manual rotate-now | M | new `rotation/triggers.rs`, `sync`, `facade.rs` |
| D7 | **Grant creation** — converge (sweep) → mint scope (fresh seed, epoch 1) → write-scope cut (name wave) → parent index update → publish → mailbox share pointer + owner sent-index | M/L | new `grants/create.rs`, D2–D5, `mailbox`, `grants/accept::SentIndex` |
| D8 | **Invites** — ephemeral-keypair grant blobs in envelope + ledger; bearer write-link flagging (host UI event); claim = ephemeral-signed mailbox request → owner converts to personal grant | M | new `grants/invite.rs`, `mailbox`, `facade.rs` |
| D9 | **Facade wiring** — `Grant`/`Revoke`/`Downgrade`/`CreateInviteLink`/`AcceptShare`/`RotateNow` command execution; abuse/bearer events out | M | `facade.rs`, `grants/*` |
| D10 | **Rehydrate-on-boot** — `ReceivedShareStore::load`/hydrate seam; cold-start rehydration of `ReceivedSharesList` from durable storage; restart-modeling test | S/M | `grants/accept.rs`, `facade.rs` |
| — | *Explicitly NOT landing:* scheduled hygiene rotation (designed-for, deferred) | — | — |

---

## 2. One PR or several? → **Decompose. This is 4–5 slices, not one PR.**

The issue is a full subsystem (~D1–D10) and its gate names ~9 distinct simulation properties. It cannot fit one agent's context, and a single PR would be un-reviewable against a fail-closed security surface. Propose five slices, each sized to one context and each blocking a **named CI gate property** from the issue's Gate section:

**Slice 1 — Grant-section record layout + direct-child-scope index (D1, D2).** *Foundation, no rotation logic.* Freezes the KAT'd wire layout and the enumeration substrate everything downstream reads. Gate: index self-heal shape; layout accept/reject KATs. **Unblocks #687** (see §3). *Parallel-safe with nothing; must land first.*

**Slice 2 — rotateScope + read-revoke/scope-exit/manual triggers (D3, part of D6).** Gate: *"trigger table and eager-set law mirrored 1:1," "immediate cut," "forgery-window bounds."* Depends on Slice 1.

**Slice 3 — sweep (D4).** Gate: *"sweep idempotence and concurrent sweepers," "crash-recovery from published records only."* Depends on Slice 1; shares eager-set enumeration with Slice 2 (sequence after 2, or parallel with a shared enumeration helper landed in Slice 1).

**Slice 4 — rotateScopeWrite + name wave + write-revoke/downgrade trigger (D5, rest of D6).** Gate: *"resumed name waves via the write-plane history link,"* three-channel re-point, inventory swap. Depends on Slices 1–2 (pointer plane, re-point object).

**Slice 5 — Grant creation + invites + facade wiring + rehydrate-on-boot (D7, D8, D9, D10).** Gate: *"grant-creation convergence requirement,"* accept-flow wiring, bearer-link flagging. Depends on Slices 1–4 (converge=sweep, write-scope cut=name wave).

Dependency order: **1 → {2, 3} → 4 → 5.** (2 and 3 can run in parallel if the eager-set/enumeration helper is landed in Slice 1.)

**Rehydrate-on-boot (D10):** fold into Slice 5, *or* pull out as a tiny standalone first slice. It only touches `accept.rs` + `facade.rs` + the seam trait and does **not** depend on any rotation work, so landing it early de-risks the facade and lets the accept-flow wiring be tested independently. Recommend: **standalone Slice 0 (S)** if the orchestrator wants early parallelism; otherwise the tail of Slice 5. Whoever owns it also owns the restart-modeling test.

---

## 3. Dependencies &amp; blockers — including the #687 question

- **Upstream:** none unmerged. #628/#632/#633 all closed and present at 6cb7d1b24.
- **#687 (grant-section `ciphertext_hash` recompute):** the marker lives at `crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs:483` — `// TODO(grant-section): recompute ciphertext_hash from record bytes, don't trust structure.input`. #687's own body says it *"depends on #633 … carries the record grant-section parsing (`envelope.unknown["grantSection"]` layout)."*

  **Correction of that assumption, load-bearing:** #633 (merged #735) did **not** land any grant-section record parsing. There is no `envelope.unknown["grantSection"]` assembler/parser and no public typed `GrantSection` in core — only a private struct in the KAT generator. The gate still consumes *separately hand-fed* decomposed structures. **The grant-section record layout that #687 recomputes over is introduced by #635 — specifically Slice 1 (D1).** So:
  - **#687 depends on #635 Slice 1, not merely on the already-closed #633.** Its "when the grant-section slice lands" clause resolves to Slice 1.
  - Recommendation: have Slice 1 either (a) do the #687 recompute inline (enumerate structures *from the record*, recompute `StructureSigInput::over_ciphertext` from the parsed sealed bytes, drop the trust of `structure.input.ciphertext_hash`) and close #687, or (b) land the layout + parser and leave the one-loop recompute to #687 as a fast follow. Given the gate is a fail-closed security surface and the recompute is cheap once the parser exists, **prefer folding #687 into Slice 1.**

---

## 4. The rehydrate-on-boot constraint — placement

Owned by **Slice 5 (or standalone Slice 0)**, per §2. Concretely it adds `async fn load(&amp;self) -&gt; SeamResult&lt;ReceivedSharesList&gt;` (or a hydrate variant) to the `ReceivedShareStore` trait in `grants/accept.rs`, calls it in `Engine::start`'s cold-start sequence in `facade.rs` to seed the in-memory `ReceivedSharesList` before any accept/scope-pointer resolve, and adds a restart-modeling test (persist N shares → drop engine → new engine over the same store → assert the list rehydrates and a redelivered mailbox item is a byte-identical no-op, not a duplicate). This closes the ack-retry deferral from #735: today the list starts empty every boot, so a persisted-but-unacked item would re-append rather than self-heal. Note the seam-contract change (a new required trait method) ripples to every host `ReceivedShareStore` impl — flag it as a breaking seam addition.

---

## 5. Determinism / seams / KAT / fail-closed surfaces to spec upfront

**Determinism (inject, never ambient):**
- All fresh seeds via the injected `Entropy` seam: read override seed, write override seed, minted scope seed (epoch 1), ephemeral invite keypair, per-version content key. No `getrandom` in rotation logic.
- Deterministic ordering: name-wave is child-first with a fixed order; sweep work-list and eager-set enumeration must order by `NodeId`'s byte `Ord` (already the documented cross-platform total order) so multi-writer/replayed runs converge.
- `Scheduler` owns sweep idle cadence, name-wave background execution (`spawn`), root-linger/migration-window timing, and the wall clock — no timers in-line.

**Seams introduced/extended:**
- `ReceivedShareStore::load` (new — D10).
- Durable persistence decisions for: direct-child-scope index, owner seed cache, `SentIndex` (are these `SnapshotCache`/`StagingStore` payloads or a new seam? — spec this before Slice 1, it shapes D2/D7).
- `Scheduler.spawn` for sweep + name-wave jobs; `RecordTransport` CAS for concurrent-sweeper safety; `Mailbox` for re-point accelerator + share pointer + invite claims; `api` client for register-first + batch-retire.

**KAT / frozen encodings (spec + accept/reject vectors from day one):**
- The grant-section record layout (D1) — the highest-value freeze; hosts and #687 depend on its exact bytes. Reject vectors: transplanted structure, duplicate tag, uncommitted structure, non-canonical CBOR.
- Structure-sig preimage recomputed over parsed ciphertext (#687).
- Re-point object `{scopeId, currentRootName, writeEpoch, minReadEpoch, prevRootName}` on all three channels — one encoding, verified identically.

**Fail-closed surfaces:**
- Eager-set **completeness** — a rotation is "not cut" until every transitively-reachable descendant scope root is republished; under-enumeration is a silent revocation hole. This is the single most security-critical assertion.
- Owner-only authority on every commitment change (`enforce_committed_ledger` already guards resolve; the *creation/revoke* side must not let a writer widen the set).
- Grant-section recompute (#687): structure `ciphertext_hash` from record bytes, structures enumerated *from the record* so completeness is gate-enforced not caller-asserted.
- Floor-law during rotation: `minReadEpoch` bumps **only** on owner-triggered read rotation; `writeEpoch` **only** on owner write rotation — grantee rotations carry no owner signature yet must not roll back a revocation boundary.
- Register-first before any wave publish; concurrent-sweeper CAS; bearer write-link flagged as a capability event.

---

## Highest-risk areas to watch

1. **Eager-set enumeration correctness** (Slices 1–2) — over/under-inclusion of transitively-reachable descendant scope roots is a revocation-security defect, not a bug. The gate's *"eager-set law mirrored 1:1"* is the assertion; the direct-child-scope index it walks must be provably complete and self-healing.
2. **Crash-recovery from published records only** (Slice 4) — no job records/checkpoints by doctrine; a resumed name wave must re-enumerate old names purely from the write-plane history link. Needs the same restart-modeling rigor as D10.
3. **Grant-section layout freeze** (Slice 1) — it's a KAT'd wire contract consumed by #687, the gate, and both hosts; getting the shape wrong is expensive to unwind. Freeze it with reject-vectors before Slices 2–5 build on it.
4. **Direct-child-scope index under concurrent moves / observed-repair** (Slice 1) — determinism + self-heal under the dest-first move semantics is subtle and shared by both sweep and rotation.
5. **Forgery-window / floor-law interplay** (Slices 2, 4) — the two independent epoch clocks (`writeEpoch` vs `minReadEpoch`) and the pointer-consult residual are the easiest place to introduce a rollback.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs (grant-section parse target; #687 TODO at line 483; `Candidate` shape)
- /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/grants/accept.rs (`ReceivedShareStore`, `ReceivedSharesList`, `SentIndex`; rehydrate seam D10)
- /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/grants/ledger.rs (`PublishedGrantBlob`, `enforce_committed_ledger`; grant-creation seam)
- /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/facade.rs (command wiring for Grant/Revoke/Downgrade/AcceptShare/RotateNow; cold-start `start`)
- /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs (all rotation sealing primitives: blobs, owner/ascent/history, commitment sign)</result>

## Report

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/741 (DRAFT) — `feat(engine): add grant-section record layout and direct-child-scope index`
**Branch:** `feat/engine-grants-section-and-child-index`
**Commit:** `ee839a5ce059d6525f4a56eb77846d2184124f81`

### Design-fork decisions
1. **Grant-section codec lives in core** (`crates/core/src/seal/section.rs`), not engine. Overrode the task's tentative engine-level default because `crates/core/src/seal/mod.rs` already explicitly reserves "the grant section" as a planned core seal slice, and the KAT regime (committed generator + manifest + native/wasm parity) lives in core. It's a pure framing codec — a peer of `write_body.rs`, no new crypto — so it's additive, not a breaking cross-crate change (did not hit the stop-condition). The engine gate composes `decode_grant_section` + the recompute.
2. **Index persists in the existing sealed write-body, no new seam.** The write-body's `direct_child_scope_index` field is already sealed under the root `writeKey`, published, gate-authenticated, and snapshot-cached. A `SnapshotCache`/`StagingStore` entry would be wrong (SnapshotCache is ciphertext-only by contract, and the write-body already *is* the canonical index). `child_index` is pure maintenance logic; durability rides the write-body. No host-seam contract change.

### Grant-section layout (frozen)
Det-CBOR map: `commitment` (encoded `GrantSetCommitment`) + `commitmentSig` (64-byte compact ECDSA); `grantBlobs` `[{tag, enc, ciphertext, sig}]`; `ownerBlob` `{enc, ciphertext, sig}`; optional `ascentLink` `{ascentPublic, enc, ciphertext, sig}`; `historyLinks` `[{sealed, sig}]`; `writeBody` `{sealed, sig}`. Each `sig` is the raw 64-byte Ed25519 structure signature. Duplicate grant-blob tag rejects `duplicate-grant-tag` with a matching release-active encode `Err` guard (encode/decode symmetry). Signature convention frozen: `H(ciphertext)` binds the HPKE `ciphertext` for grant/owner/ascent, the whole symmetric `nonce||ct||tag` for history/write-body.

**KAT vectors** (`crates/core/kat/vectors/grant/section_{accept,reject}.json`, committed-generator, manifest-pinned): accept = `full` + `vault-root-epoch-1`; reject = `duplicate-grant-blob-tag`, `missing-owner-blob`, `owner-blob-short-signature` (invalid-field-length), `commitment-sig-wrong-type`.

### #687 recompute wiring
`crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs` — the `authenticate_structure` helper (rewritten, ~line 385) recomputes `StructureSigInput::over_ciphertext(envelope.scope, envelope.epoch, struct_tag, recipient_tag, actual_ciphertext_bytes)`; stage 3 (`adopt_deferred`, ~line 476) enumerates owner blob, grant blobs, history links, write-body, and ascent link **from `candidate.grant_section`** — no caller-supplied `ciphertext_hash` is trusted, completeness is gate-enforced (owner blob + write-body mandatory at decode). `Candidate` now carries `grant_section: GrantSection`. Adversarial test: `swapped_structure_ciphertext_rejected_at_grant_section` (valid owner-pseudonym signature + swapped ciphertext → `structure-signature-invalid`).

### Direct-child-scope index (D2)
`crates/engine/src/grants/child_index.rs` — deterministic `scope_id` byte-`Ord` ordering with first-seen dedup; `insert_child`/`remove_child`/`move_child` (dest-first, orphans structurally impossible)/`repair_observed` (sweep self-heal)/`undo_dest_add`. 10 unit tests.

### Validation (all green)
`cargo fmt --all --check` (0); `cargo test -p cipherbox-core` + `-p cipherbox-engine` = **529 passing, 0 failing** (incl. grant-section KATs, 21 gate tests, 10 child_index tests); `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo build -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` finished. Review trio: crypto-privacy + security + simplify — **LOW risk, no findings requiring code changes** (fail-closed throughout, correct recompute convention, no secret leakage, deterministic).

## Independent crypto/trust-boundary review — PR #741

**Bottom line: SOUND to drive toward merge.** The #687 recompute fix is correct and the core security goal is achieved. One LOW (stale doc reference) and two informational notes; no CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM findings. Current tree is green (core+engine tests pass, wasm builds).

### Answer 1 — #687 recompute soundness: CORRECT

The vulnerability class is genuinely closed. The refactor **deleted** the old caller-supplied `SignedStructure`/`AscentCheck` types (which carried pre-computed hashes) and replaced `Candidate`'s structure fields with a single `grant_section: GrantSection` read from the record's own bytes.

- Stage 3 (`adoption.rs:459-495`) enumerates **all** seed-bearing structures — owner blob, every grant blob, every history link, write-body, ascent link — from `candidate.grant_section`, not from caller input.
- Each hash is recomputed via `StructureSigInput::over_ciphertext(scope, epoch, tag, recipient, ct)` (`adoption.rs:364-365`), where `scope`/`epoch` come from the **authenticated envelope** (`candidate.envelope.scope/.epoch`, lines 467-468) and `ct` is the **actual parsed sealed bytes**. There is no path that trusts a caller-supplied `ciphertext_hash` — `StructureSigInput` is never constructed by hand in the gate; `over_ciphertext` always calls `hash(ciphertext)` internally (`structure.rs:52-66`).
- Completeness is gate-enforced at decode: all mandatory structures use `req()` → `missing-field` on omission (`section.rs:242-262`); `ascentLink` is the only optional one, matching the spec (interior vault root omits it).
- Adversarial tests genuinely prove the fix fails without it: `swapped_structure_ciphertext_rejected_at_grant_section` (flips owner-blob ciphertext byte, keeps valid sig → `structure-signature-invalid`) and `cross_epoch_structure_replay_rejected_at_grant_section` (epoch-1 sigs in an epoch-2 envelope → rejected). Both would have adopted under the old caller-hash-trusting gate.

### Answer 2 — Structure-sig preimage convention: CONSISTENT

`H(ciphertext)` binds the HPKE `ciphertext` for grant/owner/ascent (gate passes `.ciphertext`, lines 476/483/503); the full symmetric `nonce||ct||tag` for history-link/write-body (gate passes `.sealed`, lines 488/493). The producer side (`sign_structure` in the test fixtures and `seal_*` producers) uses the identical `over_ciphertext(... same bytes)`. No encode-vs-verify mismatch — confirmed by the real-crypto round-trip in the accept matrix row and the transplant test (`structure-signature-invalid` on a history-tag sig planted in the owner-blob slot).

### Answer 3 — Encode/decode fail-closed symmetry: SATISFIED, no `assert!`/`debug_assert!`

`section.rs` contains no `assert!`/`debug_assert!`. The one semantic wire invariant (duplicate grant-blob tag) is guarded on **both** sides with the release-active, `Result`-returning `assert_grant_tags_unique` (decode `section.rs:251`, encode `section.rs:284`) — despite its name it returns `Err`, matching the `body.rs` `assert_children_unique` convention. The nested `encode_grant_set_commitment` also returns `Err` on a duplicate-tag commitment (line 285). Every other decode reject (`missing-field`, `invalid-field-length`, `unexpected-type`) is structurally unreachable from encode because it builds from typed structs with fixed-width signature/enc arrays and always-inserted required fields. Symmetry holds. Test `duplicate_grant_blob_tag_rejects_at_decode_and_encode` asserts both directions.

### Answer 4 — KAT coverage: adequate, one observation

`section_accept.json` has 2 vectors (full bundle w/ grants+ascent+history, and vault-root-epoch-1 minimal); harness asserts byte-stable re-encode + structure counts and requires the full bundle. `section_reject.json` covers `duplicate-grant-tag`, `missing-field` (missing owner blob), `invalid-field-length` (short sig), `unexpected-type` (wrong-type commitmentSig). Manifest counts match; the reject family is wired into `every_crate_check_is_pinned_by_a_vector_family`. **Native+wasm parity is automatic** — the vectors are `include_str!`'d into `kat_manifest.rs`, which is the same suite CI compiles to both targets (blueprint/core.md:222).

Observation (not a gap): the gate-level adversarial cases (swap/transplant/uncommitted/cross-epoch) live in the engine integration suite, not the core KAT — which is architecturally correct, since `section.rs` is a pure framing codec with no crypto to KAT. No non-canonical-CBOR reject vector is in this family, but canonical-form enforcement is exercised by the shared codec KAT (`non-canonical-uint`/`non-canonical-length`), so it is not re-litigated per-structure.

### Answer 5 — Write-body wire-format change: NO CHANGE (author's claim confirmed)

`crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs` is **not** in this PR's diff. `ChildScopeRef` and `WriteBody.direct_child_scope_index` pre-existed (introduced #675, hardened #726). `child_index.rs` is pure maintenance/ordering logic over `Vec&lt;ChildScopeRef&gt;` riding the already-sealed, already-KAT'd write-body field — no new seam, no format change, no backward-compat break. This is not a finding.

### Answer 6 — Trust boundary / determinism / secret hygiene: CLEAN

- All decode paths return `Result` via `?`/`req`/`bytes_fixed` — no `unwrap`/panic on attacker bytes; allocations are bounded by the (upstream-bounded) record size, no length-prefix pre-alloc attack (canonical CBOR reads actual bytes).
- No secret leaks: `Signed*`/`GrantSection` `Debug` expose only ciphertext/signatures/public halves. Gate-recovered seeds are `SecretBytes` (zeroize on drop, gate is terminal owner); `reader.read_key` is borrowed and never zeroized (correct per rule 7). No `format!`/log of key material.
- Determinism: `canonicalize` orders by `scope_id` byte `Ord` (`child_index.rs:56`), permutation-independent; no clock/RNG anywhere.

### Answer 7 — Collision-recovery integrity: CLEAN except one stale doc line

The 4 touched wiring files (`seal/mod.rs`, `gate/mod.rs`, `grants/mod.rs`, `lib.rs`) consistently add the new exports and remove the deleted `AscentCheck`/`SignedStructure`. A repo-wide grep for orphaned references to removed types/old `Candidate` fields found **exactly one**, and it's a doc comment, not code (see LOW below). `grants_mailbox.rs` and `accept.rs` were fully migrated to `grant_section.commitment`. No half-reverted or reformatted-not-re-edited hunks.

**LOW — stale rustdoc reference to a removed field**
`crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs:265` — the `parent_node_seed` doc says "Required whenever `candidate.ascent` is `Some`", but the field was renamed in this very PR to `candidate.grant_section.ascent_link`. No functional impact; fix the reference to avoid a dangling doc pointer.
*Suggested fix:* change `` `candidate.ascent` `` → `` `candidate.grant_section.ascent_link` ``.

**INFO — extra top-level structures are preserved, not rejected**
`decode_grant_section` routes unknown top-level fields to `unknown` (forward-compat tolerance) rather than rejecting. This is safe by design: the gate authenticates only the known enumerated structures, so an attacker cannot smuggle an unauthenticated seed-bearing structure via an unknown field. Called out only so it is a conscious decision, not an oversight.

**INFO — no grant-blob-vs-commitment tag cross-check in the gate**
A committed writer's grant blob to a tag not in the commitment is authenticated (valid structure sig) but not cross-checked against commitment entries here. Correct for slice 1 — that authority check is the ledger/accept path's job (`enforce_committed_ledger`, accept.rs), and the sealed seed only opens for the tag's key holder. Not a defect.

Verdict: **#741 is sound; drive it toward merge.** The single LOW doc nit can be folded in or deferred at the author's discretion — it has no security or behavioral impact.</result>

## Report

### Greptile P2 — verbatim
&gt; **[P2][security] Grant Arrays Have No Limit** (`crates/core/src/seal/section.rs:248`)
&gt; "A resolved record can supply arbitrarily large `grantBlobs` and `historyLinks` arrays. Decoding allocates every entry, and adoption then hashes and verifies each structure, so an oversized canonical record can consume excessive memory and CPU before rejection; enforce protocol-level cardinality limits while decoding both arrays."

### Disposition: NOT folded — out-of-scope for this frozen codec (false-positive on the amplification claim; the residual large-input concern belongs elsewhere)
Verified against current code. The proposed fix is misplaced and would touch a frozen format:

1. **No allocation amplification.** The det-CBOR array decoder (`codec/decode.rs` MAJOR_ARRAY, lines 166-172) uses `Vec::new()` and pushes one element per iteration — each element requires `read_value` to consume ≥1 real input byte. `take_len` (140-148) rejects any length claim exceeding remaining input *before* allocating. The decoder deliberately never `with_capacity`-preallocates from the declared array header, so cardinality is linearly bounded by the record's own byte length. There is no small-record → huge-allocation vector — the premise "decoding allocates every entry" is only true proportional to bytes actually received.
2. **Work is linear in bytes transmitted.** To make adoption hash/verify N structures an attacker must ship an O(N)-sized record. That's a generic large-input concern whose bound belongs at the resolved-record fetch boundary (a max-record-size cap on the IPFS-fetched envelope content), not the wire codec, and isn't specific to grant arrays. Note the grant section rides in IPFS-fetched envelope content, not the IPNS record itself.
3. **Frozen KAT'd format.** A fixed cardinality cap would change a FROZEN, KAT-covered wire format and impose a protocol max on recipients (`grantBlobs`) and key-regression epochs (`historyLinks`). `blueprint/core.md` specifies no such limit; the core codec has no cardinality-cap precedent (only `MAX_DEPTH=128` for nesting). Adding an arbitrary cap without blueprint authority risks fail-closed rejection of legitimate large grant-sets.

Per the "stop rather than force a fix that ripples beyond #741" instruction, I did **not** add a cap. I replied with this rationale and resolved the thread. Recommended follow-up (out of scope): a max-resolved-record-size cap at the fetch boundary — flag for the orchestrator if you want an issue filed.

### Frozen layout / KAT: not touched. No wire-format, KAT vector, or manifest change.

### LOW doc fix: landed
`crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs` — stale `candidate.ascent` rustdoc reference changed to `candidate.grant_section.ascent_link`. Verified the field path exists (`Candidate.grant_section: GrantSection` at :248, `GrantSection.ascent_link` used at :496). Doc-only, zero behavioral impact.

## Report: PR #741 review resolution

**Findings &amp; dispositions**

1. **CodeRabbit — Major, `child_index.rs` canonicalize duplicate resolution** (thread `PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6S5nUR`): **Partial fold.** The claim that permutation-independence breaks for same-`scope_id`/differing-content entries is technically true but describes an input the domain invariant forbids ("a scope_id names exactly one child") and the maintenance ops never emit. CR's proposed full-entry tie-break is **infeasible** (`ChildScopeRef.unknown` is `Vec&lt;(String, Value)&gt;` and `codec::Value` implements no `Ord`) and **wrong** (it would override the deliberate replace-wins policy — `insert_child` prepends the authoritative entry so a fresh add wins over a stale duplicate). <result>Done. Slice 1.5 — the eager-set enumeration walk — is implemented, validated, and opened as a draft PR.

## Summary

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/744 (DRAFT, base `main`)
**Branch:** `feat/engine-eager-set-enumeration`
**Commit:** `f6dd24b80`

### What was built
The transitive **eager set** (F-4 cascade) enumeration — `enumerate_eager_set(root_scope_id, root_child_index, resolver)` — in a new `crates/engine/src/rotation/` module (`eager_set.rs` + `mod.rs`, registered in `lib.rs`). A pure, deterministic BFS: level-1 is the root's caller-held `direct_child_scope_index`; deeper levels come from each descendant's own write-body index via an injected seam. Returns `EagerSet` (descendants only — the rotator holds/rotates the root itself), ordered by `scope_id` byte `Ord`.

### Seam design + placement
`ChildIndexResolver::direct_child_index(child) -&gt; Result&lt;Vec&lt;ChildScopeRef&gt;, ResolveFailure&gt;` abstracts "resolve + gate + unseal a descendant's write-body to read its child index" — the walk's only impure edge (no clock/RNG/IO in the traversal). It is a **rotation-module trait, not a new host seam** (no `SeamSet` change), faked in-slice; real wiring is a later slice's job. Placement in `rotation/` per the task default, where Slices 2/3 will live.

### Fail-closed-completeness semantics chosen
**Hard fail-closed.** Any reachable descendant whose index can't be authoritatively obtained aborts the walk with `EnumerationError { scope_id, reason }` (`ResolveFailure::Rejected` vs `Unavailable`, mirroring the gate's `GateError::Rejected` vs `Seam`). The walk never returns a partial set — `EagerSet` has no public constructor other than a successful walk, so an incomplete cascade is unrepresentable. Rationale: eager-set completeness is the revocation guarantee; a missed descendant is a silent revocation hole (blueprint/engine.md eager-set law #26 D2 / #38 D5, "cached descendant seeds are why ascent-re-seal alone is insufficient"; AGENTS.md security rule 6). This is the stricter of the two options the task allowed. Termination via a `scope_id`-keyed visited set; no arbitrary cap (first-forgery abort bounds any injected fan-out).

### Tests
- Completeness: `completeness_enumerates_multi_level_tree`, `completeness_wide_fanout`, `completeness_diamond_shared_descendant_recorded_once`, `every_descendant_including_leaves_is_resolved`
- Cycle-safety/termination: `cycle_back_edge_terminates`, `self_loop_terminates`, `back_edge_to_root_is_ignored_root_excluded`
- Determinism: `determinism_order_is_scope_id_ascending`, `determinism_permutation_independent_output`
- Fail-closed: `fail_closed_on_rejected_descendant_names_scope_and_reason`, `fail_closed_on_unavailable_descendant`, `fail_closed_first_failure_bounds_resolves`

### Validation (all green)
`cargo fmt --all`; `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` (22+7+7+3+17+17+15 pass) and `-p cipherbox-core` (154+58+… pass); `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo build -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` builds.

### Review trio folded
Simplify: deduped a local hex helper onto the existing `api::signer::hex_lower` (made `mod signer` → `pub(crate) mod signer`, completing that helper's existing `pub(crate)` intent). Security + crypto-privacy (manual rigorous pass — diff is a single self-contained traversal module): no crypto material touched (the walk moves only public `scope_id`/`ipnsName`), no wire path, no secret bytes, no key logging; `EnumerationError` Display emits only a non-secret scope UUID + generic reason. Trust-critical property (fail-closed completeness) enforced and tested. No findings beyond the reuse cleanup.

One note: the initial `git checkout -b` was bundled into a command the worktree guard refused (it also had `env --json`), so the branch was created separately just before push — no impact on the result.</result>

## CRYPTO / TRUST REVIEW — PR #744 (eager-set enumeration walk, #635 Slice 1.5)

Branch `feat/engine-eager-set-enumeration` @ `f6dd24b8`. Files reviewed: `crates/engine/src/rotation/eager_set.rs` (new, the walk), `crates/engine/src/rotation/mod.rs`, `lib.rs`/`api/mod.rs` exports, plus dependencies `grants/child_index.rs::canonicalize`, `core::seal::write_body::{ChildScopeRef, WriteBody}`, `api/signer::hex_lower`, `testkit::block_on`.

### Verdict: the walk is SOUND on all seven checklist axes. No CONFIRMED under-enumeration, mis-ordering, non-termination, or secret-leak defect. Findings below are one PLAUSIBLE seam-contract residual (the strongest risk) and two minor test/doc gaps. No code changes recommended as blocking.

---

### Per-axis confirmation

1. COMPLETENESS — SOUND. Level-1 is the caller-held `root_child_index` (canonicalized into the initial frontier); every deeper level is pulled from each descendant's OWN index via `resolver.direct_child_index(child)` (`eager_set.rs:308`). Visit and full child-expansion are atomic (`eager_set.rs:296-306`): a node is recorded in `discovered` and its children pushed to `next` in the same iteration, so no discovered node is left unexplored. Diamonds/back-edges are the only things skipped, and only after the node was already fully explored via its first (shallower/canonically-earlier) encounter. No branch, filter, or early-return drops a reachable distinct `scope_id`.

2. FAIL-CLOSED — SOUND. Every resolver error (`Rejected` and `Unavailable`) is `map_err`→`?` (`eager_set.rs:303-309`), aborting with `EnumerationError`. `EagerSet` is constructed only at the single terminal `Ok(...)` after the `while` fully drains (`eager_set.rs:314`); there is no early `Ok`, no `Default`, and the `descendants` field is private with no public/`pub(crate)` constructor. A partial set is genuinely unrepresentable.

3. TERMINATION / CYCLE — SOUND. `visited: BTreeSet&lt;[u8;16]&gt;` pre-seeded with `root_scope_id` (`eager_set.rs:283`); `visited.insert(child.scope_id)` gates all work (`eager_set.rs:297`). Self-loop, A→B→A cycle, diamond, and back-edge-to-root all terminate (tests `cycle_back_edge_terminates`, `self_loop_terminates`, `back_edge_to_root_is_ignored_root_excluded`). No unbounded fan-out: a forged child fails its own resolve and aborts the whole walk (`fail_closed_first_failure_bounds_resolves`). The absence of a depth/count cap is correct — a legitimately large owner tree must not be rejected, and only real gate-passing scopes cost resolves.

4. DETERMINISM — SOUND. Output is `discovered.into_values()` from a `BTreeMap&lt;[u8;16], _&gt;` → ascending `scope_id`. Each level is `canonicalize`d before descent (`eager_set.rs:311`); first-encounter recording (`visited.insert` guard) means the recorded entry for a shared descendant is the canonically-first parent's, fixed under input permutation. No clock/RNG/HashMap in the production path (the test `FakeResolver`'s `HashMap` never affects output order). `determinism_permutation_independent_output` proves byte-identical convergence.

5. SECRET HANDLING — SOUND. The walk touches only `ChildScopeRef{scope_id, ipns_name}` — both public. `EnumerationError`/`ResolveFailure` `Display` emit `hex_lower(scope_id)` (public 16-byte id) and static reason strings; no seed/read-key/writeKey material anywhere in Debug/Display/error/log. `EagerSet`/`ChildScopeRef` `Debug` derive over public fields only.

6. SEAM CORRECTNESS — see finding R1.

7. TEST ADEQUACY — SOUND. All 14 tests are behavioral (assert outputs/call-counts/error identity), none assert source text. The checklist's named adversarial cases are all present: descendant index containing the root (`back_edge_to_root_...`), resolver child pointing back up (`cycle_back_edge_...`), shared descendant resolved once (`diamond_...` with `calls==3`), first-failure bound (`fail_closed_first_failure_bounds_resolves`). See R2 for the one gap.

---

### R1 — PLAUSIBLE (strongest residual): the `ChildIndexResolver` contract does not oblige scope_id↔record-identity binding

`crates/engine/src/rotation/eager_set.rs:258-269` (trait doc) and the whole walk key `visited`/`discovered`/rotation identity on the **parent-supplied** `child.scope_id`. I confirmed `WriteBody` (`crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:169-178`) carries **no** self-identifying scope/node id — only its `direct_child_scope_index`. So a resolved+gated child write-body cannot be cross-checked against the `scope_id` label the parent index asserted for it. The IPNS adoption gate binds `ipns_name → record` (Ed25519 pubkey from the name), but nothing binds `scope_id → that record`. The `scope_id` label's integrity therefore rests **entirely** on the trusted-writer authorship of the parent's sealed+gated write-body index.

Within the stated trust model (parent write-body sealed under the parent's `writeKey`, gate-authenticated) this is acceptable and is NOT a defect in this slice — the walk correctly delegates identity to the gated parent index. The residual risk is a **seam-contract underspecification** that a later slice could silently violate: the doc says only "resolve+gate+unseal … yield its `direct_child_scope_index`," leaving implicit that (a) `ipns_name` is the sole gated identity edge and (b) `scope_id` is a trusted parent-supplied label the resolver must not let an attacker influence independently of the gated parent record. Failure scenario a future resolver could introduce: a resolver that accepts a `scope_id` from any network-supplied source other than the gated parent index (e.g. a registry hint) would let a gate-passing-but-mislabeled entry dedup/rotate the wrong scope key — a completeness hole the current tests (which fake the resolver) cannot catch. Per the #682 "audit the binding surface upfront" learning, recommend making this an explicit contract clause on `direct_child_index` + a resolver-side binding test in the wiring slice.

### R2 — MINOR (test gap): first-seen *content* determinism is unverified

Determinism tests use identical child content. There is no test asserting **which** `ChildScopeRef` (i.e. which `ipns_name`) is recorded when the same `scope_id` is discovered via two parents carrying differing `ipns_name`. The code does resolve this deterministically (canonically-first parent / shallowest level wins, via the `visited.insert` first-encounter guard + stable-sort `canonicalize`), but the guarantee is untested. A differing-`ipns_name` diamond test would lock it in. Low severity — the mechanism is sound.

### R3 — NIT (doc imprecision, non-blocking): the module comment (`eager_set.rs:41-44` region) claims a forged fan-out costs "at most one wasted resolve." Precisely, up to the canonical position of the first forgery within a frontier can be resolved, bounded by the number of genuinely gate-passing scopes an attacker controls. The security bound (first-failure abort) holds; the phrasing understates the count. Mentioned only for accuracy, not a finding.

---

### Bottom line
The enumeration walk is provably complete, fail-closed, terminating, deterministic, and secret-clean as written. The only thing standing between this and a silent revocation hole in the shipped product is the **deferred resolver's** faithful implementation of "gate the `ipns_name`, treat `scope_id` as a trusted parent-index label" — that binding obligation should be written into the `ChildIndexResolver` contract now (R1) so the wiring slice's tests are forced to cover it. Relevant file: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/rotation/eager_set.rs`.</result>

**Commit:** `b9dced0ee88bff01b404a97ea67c67147e83aaec` — `docs(engine): harden eager-set resolver contract and lock first-seen content`
Pushed `f6dd24b80..b9dced0ee` to `origin/feat/engine-eager-set-enumeration`. Single file changed: `crates/engine/src/rotation/eager_set.rs` (+62/-3). PR #744 left as draft, not merged.

**R1 — binding-contract clause** added to the `ChildIndexResolver::direct_child_index` doc, referencing issue #745 (unescaped, as instructed). Exact text of the added clause:

&gt; `# Binding contract`
&gt;
&gt; `ipns_name` is the **sole gated identity edge**: the adoption gate binds `ipns_name -&gt; record` via the Ed25519 pubkey derived from the name. `scope_id` is a **trusted parent-index label** carried inside the parent's sealed + gated write-body index. The real resolver MUST derive each descendant's `scope_id` solely from that gated parent index entry, and MUST NEVER let a network-supplied source (e.g. a registry hint) influence `scope_id` independently of the gated parent record. The walk keys visited / dedup / rotation identity on `scope_id`, so an independently influenced `scope_id` would dedup or rotate the wrong scope key — a silent revocation hole defeating the eager-set completeness guarantee. Enforcement of this obligation in the real resolver-wiring slice is tracked in #745.

**R2 — determinism test** added: `determinism_diamond_records_first_seen_ipns_name` (plus a `child_named` helper). Builds a diamond where scope `0x04` is reachable via parents `0x01`/`0x02` carrying differing ipns_names (`via-a`/`via-b`), asserts the recorded ChildScopeRef is the canonically-first parent's (`via-a`) and is byte-identical under root-index permutation. Behavioral asserts only, public scope_id/ipns_name material only.

**R3 — bounding comment** tightened: the forged fan-out bound now reads "at most the scopes ordered before the first forgery's canonical position are resolved (bounded by the genuinely gate-passing scopes the attacker controls), never an unbounded injected fan-out."

|---|----------|----------|---------|---------|

### Detail

**Finding 3 (accepted)** — fold in commit `a4dea7f9a`:
- New neutral private `crate::hex` module (`crates/engine/src/hex.rs`) holding `hex_lower` + its test.
- Reverted `api/mod.rs` back to `mod signer;` (undid the `pub(crate)` widening).
- Repointed `signer.rs` and `rotation/eager_set.rs` at `crate::hex::hex_lower`. `signer` stays scoped to `api`; rotation no longer couples to the signing module.

**Finding 2 (declined)** — the walk is already bounded by design: O(distinct gate-passing scope count) via the `scope_id` visited set, hard-aborts at the first gate failure (so an attacker can't amplify — covered by `fail_closed_first_failure_bounds_resolves`), and a fixed depth/count cap would be a fail-open regression since the module must not reject a legitimately large owner tree (a capped walk would silently under-enumerate = revocation hole).

**Finding 1 (deferred)** — valid concern but resolution belongs to the resolver-binding slice, not this pure walk. The walk isn't wired to a real resolver yet (no live rotation path, no live hole), and whether a `scope_id`/`ipns_name` conflict can even arise is fixed by the gated binding semantics owned by #745. Flipping the reviewed first-seen determinism (R2) to a hard-abort should happen with that binding context. # Readiness verdict: #635 Slice 2 (rotateScope) &amp; Slice 3 (sweep)

## Ground truth established
- **No rotate/sweep/pending-rotation type exists anywhere yet.** Repo-wide grep for `rotate_scope|sweep|RotationRecord|pending_rotation|epoch_lag` returns only `crates/engine/src/grants/revocation.rs:35` (`ResolutionClass::EpochLag`) and the merged `rotation/eager_set.rs`. Both slices are net-new code under `crates/engine/src/rotation/`.
- **The blueprint forbids a shared rotation-record store.** `blueprint/engine.md:235-239` — "no recovery machinery: no job records, no checkpoints — published records are the sole source of truth." The sweep work-list is "the epoch-lag predicate" over published records (`engine.md:269`), not a queue handed off by rotateScope.

## Slice 2 — rotateScope + trigger surface

**Needs** (`engine.md:241-265`, triggers `:294-308`): mint a random override seed at the scope root; re-seal the eager set (owner blob, ascent link, history link, grant blobs re-wrapped verbatim for the committed set); structure-sign each with the writer pseudonym; publish; bump `minReadEpoch`; enqueue the sweep. Triggers: read-revoke (blob+ledger+commitment entry removal), full-depth scope-exit, manual rotate-now.

**Already on the post-#744 surface — all core crypto exists (rotation is composition, not new crypto):**
- `crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs`: `seal_grant_blob:235`, `seal_owner_blob:367`, `seal_ascent_link:456`, `encode_history_link_payload:585`, `OverrideSeedPayload:282`.
- `crates/core/src/seal/section.rs` `GrantSection` (Slice 1 #741) — assembles the re-sealed scope-root record.
- KDF catalog `crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs:63-130` (node-seed, read-key, structure-key, ascent-keypair, pseudonym-sign, pointer-read-key). **The epoch bump / root cut needs NO new KDF edge** — override seeds are "random at rotation... none derive through here" (`kdf/mod.rs:19-20`); the epoch is a plain `u64` field on `OverrideSeedPayload`.
- Eager-set enumeration `rotation/eager_set.rs:191` (`enumerate_eager_set`).
- Ledger/commitment/revocation primitives for the read-revoke trigger: `grants/ledger.rs`, `grants/revocation.rs`, core grant-set commitment.
- Seams for the republish path: `FloorStore::raise_epoch_floor` (`seams/floor_store.rs:29`) for the minReadEpoch bump, `Scheduler::spawn` for "enqueue the sweep", `RecordTransport`/`net/publish` for CAS republish, `net/resolve` `Adopter`.

**Missing / built in-slice:** the `rotateScope` orchestration itself, the trigger table, and a **"re-seal one scope root at epoch N and publish"** helper (no such helper exists — grep for `reseal|republish_scope` finds only `net/liveness.rs:95 eol_republish`, unrelated).

**Real-resolver dependency:** **NO.** rotateScope calls `enumerate_eager_set(root_id, root_index, resolver)` against the **injected** `ChildIndexResolver` seam (`eager_set.rs:158-180`), exactly as Slice 1.5 did. The real network resolve/gate/unseal wiring is explicitly "a later slice's / the facade's job" (`eager_set.rs:156-157`), and the #635 gate is **engine simulation** with a faked resolver. #745/#746 do **not** block building or simulation-testing this slice.

### Slice 2 readiness: **READY**

## Slice 3 — sweep

**Needs** (`engine.md:267-278`): idempotent epoch-lag work-list over published records; metadata-only re-seal of lagging descendants (regressed to the cut epoch via history link — **no fresh seed minted**); direct-child-scope index self-heal; idle-cadence `Scheduler` job; CAS-safe concurrent sweepers.

**Already present:** epoch-lag substrate — `FloorStore::epoch_floor` (`seams/floor_store.rs:25`) vs envelope record epoch; index self-heal `grants/child_index.rs` `repair_observed` (Slice 1); `Scheduler::spawn`/`sleep` for idle cadence; `RecordTransport` CAS for concurrent-sweeper safety; `enumerate_eager_set` to walk the subtree.

**Missing / built in-slice:** the sweep orchestration, the epoch-lag predicate, and the **same "re-seal a scope root and publish" helper** Slice 2 needs (sweep re-seals metadata-only under the already-advanced epoch).

**Real-resolver dependency:** **NO** — same as Slice 2; injected `ChildIndexResolver` + faked seams, simulation gate. Not blocked on #745/#746.

### Slice 3 readiness: **READY**

## Parallelism verdict

**4. File-disjointness:** Mostly disjoint but **not cleanly.** Slice 2 → `rotation/rotate.rs` (+ triggers), Slice 3 → `rotation/sweep.rs`. Both edit `rotation/mod.rs` (trivial module-decl merge). The real collision is a **shared scope-root re-seal/publish helper** both slices require — which exists nowhere today.

**5. Data dependency:** **No shared pending-rotation record type** (blueprint forbids it — `engine.md:235`; sweep reads epoch-lag from published records, `:269`). **But there is a shared code dependency:** the "assemble → structure-sign → publish a scope-root `GrantSection`" primitive. rotateScope re-seals the full eager set with fresh seeds; sweep re-seals a lagging node metadata-only — different seed source, same publish machinery. This is shared **function T**, not a shared data type.

**Parallelizable?** **YES-AFTER** — land the shared scope-root re-seal/publish helper `T` first, then 2 and 3 run truly in parallel. Absent that, two agents will both author `T` and collide/duplicate.

**6. Hidden prerequisites:** None missing beyond `T`. Floor-law persistence seam exists (`FloorStore`); a pending-rotation store is explicitly forbidden; the **name wave is not a dependency** — that's write-plane `rotateScopeWrite` (`engine.md:280-292`), a separate Slice 4 primitive. Slice 2 is read-plane only (bumps `minReadEpoch`, not `writeEpoch`).

## Recommendation

- **Prerequisite to land before either starts:** a tiny Slice 1.75 — the shared `reseal_and_publish_scope_root(seed_source, committed_set, writer_pseudonym) -&gt; GrantSection` helper (composition over existing `crates/core` seal primitives + `RecordTransport` CAS publish). Small, pure-composition, unblocks both.
- **Then run Slice 2 and Slice 3 in parallel** — file-disjoint once `T` exists, no shared-type dependency, both build against the injected `ChildIndexResolver` and faked seams. **Neither is blocked on #745/#746** (those gate only the future live network resolver, outside #635's simulation gate).
- **Fallback if you skip 1.75:** serialize 2→3 (Slice 2 defines `T`, Slice 3 consumes it). Do **not** launch 2 and 3 as blind parallel agents without `T` — guaranteed collision on the re-seal helper.</result>

## Summary

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/747 (DRAFT) · **Branch:** `feat/engine-rotate-scope` · **Head SHA:** `6459ca1c59ace01496590f4c7d5e7271357f6084`

### What landed (3 new files under `crates/engine/src/rotation/`)

**`reseal.rs` — the shared re-seal helper** (the Slice-3-consumable primitive). Public signature:
```rust
pub fn reseal_scope_root&lt;E: Entropy&gt;(
    entropy: &amp;mut E,
    identity: &amp;ScopeRootIdentity&lt;'_&gt;,
    seeds: &amp;ResealSeeds&lt;'_&gt;,
    committed: &amp;CommittedSet&lt;'_&gt;,
    carried_history_links: &amp;[SignedSealed],
) -&gt; Result&lt;GrantSection, ResealError&gt;
```
Composes `crates/core` seal primitives into a signed `GrantSection` (grant blobs re-wrapped for the committed set, owner blob, ascent link, history links, sealed write-body — all pseudonym-signed). Seed source is parameterised via `ResealSeeds` so rotate (fresh seed + new epoch + `prev`) and the sweep (existing seed, `prev = None`, no new history link) reuse it.

**`rotate.rs` — `rotate_scope`** (the read-plane root cut) + the `ScopeRootPublisher` seam (faked in-slice, real content-plane/`net::publish` CAS wiring = #745/#746).

**`trigger.rs` — `revoke_read_grant`** (owner-only committed-set cut) + `RotationTrigger` classifier.

### How I divided eager re-seal vs deferred sweep
Per `engine.md` L241-266 (rotateScope eager set) and L268-278 (sweep) + CONTEXT.md: the eager/lazy boundary is **scope-root vs interior node**, not root vs descendant — no ambiguity. This slice lands the re-seal primitive + the root cut + triggers; the eager-set *descendant scope roots* (enumerated by #744) are re-sealed through the same helper by the cascade orchestration once #745/#746 provide per-descendant parent-seed material. I did **not** guess the tree/parent-seed threading — it needs the deferred resolver wiring.

### Trigger table
| Trigger | Committed change | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Scope exit | none (grantee flat) | `rotate_scope`, unchanged set |
| Read revoke | revokee removed | `revoke_read_grant` → `rotate_scope` |
| Manual | none | `rotate_scope` |

### Effect ordering (crash-safety)
**publish CAS → raise `minReadEpoch` → enqueue sweep**, aborting before the next on any failure. Publish-before-floor avoids the lockout failure mode (a floor demanding an unpublished epoch); a failed publish/lost race raises no floor and enqueues no sweep. Documented in the PR body and module docs.

### Key test coverage (30 new tests, all green)
- **`revoked_grantee_is_absent_survivors_present_at_new_epoch`** — the revocation guarantee: revokee's blob absent, survivors re-wrapped at the new epoch, revokee cannot open survivors, survivor decrypts the fresh seed.
- **`diverging_ledger_fails_closed_release_active`** + **`unusable_recipient_key_fails_closed`** — release-active encode-side guards (runtime `Err`, not `debug_assert!`).
- `publish_failure_does_not_bump_floor_or_enqueue_sweep` (lockout guard), `mints_a_fresh_override_seed_distinct_from_the_current`, full-structure round-trip + signature verification, determinism, vault-root/sweep seed-source variants.

# Crypto/Trust Review — PR #747 `rotateScope` (read-plane key-regression rotation)

Scope reviewed in full: `crates/engine/src/rotation/{reseal.rs, rotate.rs, trigger.rs, mod.rs}`, `lib.rs`, plus the composed primitives (`grants/ledger.rs::enforce_committed_ledger`, `core/seal/{grant.rs,section.rs}` encode guards, `seams/floor_store.rs`, the floor-store fake).

Bottom line: rotation is **sound on every core security axis** — revocation completeness, fresh-seed entropy, effect ordering/crash-safety, encode fail-closed symmetry, and secret hygiene all hold. No fail-open, no seed leak, no revoked-grantee survivor. Findings are residual-risk and hardening/test-gap items, none blocking on their own, but two (F1, F2) must be honored by the follow-on cascade wiring.

## Axis-by-axis verdict

- **1. Revocation completeness — SOUND.** `reseal_scope_root` calls `enforce_committed_ledger` *before* any seal (reseal.rs:268), then wraps exactly one grant blob per surviving ledger entry. `revoke_read_grant` removes the revokee from **both** commitment and ledger and owner-re-signs (trigger.rs:1570-1590). Owner blob and ascent link wrap the **new** seed; the history link seals the **prev** seed under `structure_key(new_seed)` (reseal.rs:357-361) — the ratchet is forward-only, so a revoked holder of the old seed cannot open the history link to derive the new seed. The crown-jewel test proves the revokee has no blob and cannot open a survivor's re-wrap. No path leaves a revoked entry in blobs/owner-blob/ledger/commitment.
- **2. Fresh-seed entropy — SOUND.** Seed minted only via the injected `entropy.fill` into a `Zeroizing` buffer (rotate.rs:1133-1136); ephemerals/nonces likewise from the same seam. No direct RNG/clock. Epoch is `current+1`.
- **3. Effect ordering / crash-safety — SOUND.** Order is publish → `raise_epoch_floor` → `spawn(sweep)` with `?`-abort between each (rotate.rs:1168-1181). Floor is raised only after a confirmed publish and only to `new_read_epoch`, which is exactly the published record's epoch — so no floor ever demands an unpublished epoch (no lockout), and the floor never fails to rise while leaving a new epoch un-gated on the *completing* run. `FloorStore` is structurally monotonic-max, so replay of an old epoch is rejected and a re-run is idempotent-safe. `LostRace`/`NotPublished` both leave the floor untouched — clean no-op.
- **4. Encode fail-closed symmetry — SOUND.** The dup-grant-tag invariant that `decode_grant_section` rejects is mirrored release-active in `encode_grant_section` via `assert_grant_tags_unique` returning `Err` (section.rs:284, body.rs:442-451) — no `debug_assert`. `encode_write_body` is propagated as `ResealError::WriteBodyEncode` (release-active). The up-front `enforce_committed_ledger` is the encode-side mirror of the gate's resolve check.
- **5. Secret hygiene — SOUND.** `new_override_seed` is `Zeroizing` (terminal owner). Borrowed seeds are never zeroed by the callee. Grant/owner/ascent/history payloads hold seeds in `SecretBytes` (zeroize-on-drop) and each seal primitive zeroizes its plaintext buffer (grant.rs:249,381,472,612). No secret bytes in any `Display`/`format!`/`{:?}`/log; error `check()`/Display carry only classifier strings and `CodecError.check()`. Tests use `ct_eq_32` for secret equality.
- **7. Helper reuse (rotate vs sweep) — SOUND.** `prev = None` cleanly yields no fresh history link, carried links verbatim, same epoch (reseal.rs:355-368; `sweep_seed_source_mints_no_new_history_link` proves it). No coupling forces the sweep to mint a seed or bump an epoch.

## Findings (most severe first)

### F1 — [RESIDUAL, HIGH if mis-wired] `rotate_scope` cuts only the root; the eager-set descendant cascade is deferred
`rotate.rs` re-cuts a single scope root and never touches the `enumerate_eager_set` walk (no resolver param; grep confirms zero references). `trigger.rs`'s own table advertises ReadRevoke → "full eager-set cascade," but the wired path (`revoke_read_grant` → `rotate_scope`) only rotates the root. That is fine *as a primitive* and the module docs are explicit that #745/#746 land the descendant cascade — but it means **a read-revoke driven end-to-end on the root cut alone leaves every descendant scope root on its old override seed**, so a revoked party who previously cached/resolved a descendant's seed still reads it. Failure scenario: owner shares folder F (with subfolder G) to Bob, Bob resolves G (caches G's seed), owner revokes Bob and only the root cut runs → Bob still opens G. This is the whole reason the eager set exists. Action: do **not** expose read-revoke as complete until the cascade wiring lands, and keep the trigger table's "full cascade" claim from being read as satisfied by this slice.

### F2 — [PLAUSIBLE, MEDIUM] `reseal_scope_root` does not bind `identity.pseudonym_signer` to `commitment.owner_pseudonym_pk`
Every structure is signed with `identity.pseudonym_signer` (reseal.rs:280-282), but nothing checks `pseudonym_signer.verifying_key().to_bytes() == commitment.owner_pseudonym_pk`. The resolve/adoption gate verifies structure signatures against the committed `owner_pseudonym_pk`; a caller that wires the wrong signer (very plausible once the #745/#746 cascade re-seals many descendants, each with its own pseudonym, through this one shared helper) silently produces a section **every gate rejects** — an unopenable scope root. This is fail-*closed* (a liveness/lockout hazard, not a revocation bypass), but it is exactly the encode/decode-symmetry class AGENTS rule 8 targets: the decoder hard-rejects a bad-signer structure, so the encoder should refuse to mint one. Recommend a release-active `Err` guard (e.g. `ResealError::SignerNotCommitted`) at the top of `reseal_scope_root`, with a release-active test.

### F3 — [CONFIRMED, LOW] The `RotateError::Floor` path is untested
`InMemoryFloorStore::raise_epoch_floor` always returns `Ok` (fakes/floor_store.rs), and no failing-floor fake exists, so the publish-succeeded / floor-raise-failed branch (rotate.rs:1175-1178) — the precise crash window the module docs foreground ("record is published (no lockout); the cut completes on retry") — has zero coverage. The happy-path snapshot proves publish-before-floor, and publish-failure/lost-race are covered, but the floor-failure ordering claim is asserted only in prose. Add a scripted failing-`FloorStore` test asserting `RotateError::Floor`, record published, `spawned == 0`.

### F4 — [PLAUSIBLE, LOW] `enforce_committed_ledger` checks only `(tag → permission)`, not `recipient_enc_pk`
`reseal_scope_root` wraps each grant blob to `entry.recipient_enc_pk` taken verbatim from the ledger, but `enforce_committed_ledger` (ledger.rs:103-125) compares only tag→permission sets. A write-grantee re-sealer (scope-exit/manual path) could keep a survivor's tag+permission while swapping its `recipient_enc_pk`, and this guard would pass. The tag↔enc_pk binding (blinded-tag self-location) is enforced only on resolve, which is #745/#746 work not yet landed. At worst this misdirects a survivor's blob (DoS / redirect to an attacker key that still can't self-locate the tag), not a revocation bypass — but **#745/#746 must not assume reseal validated enc_pk binding**, and reseal has no independent guard. Flagging so the binding responsibility is explicit at the seam.

### F5 — [FOOTNOTE, negligible] Epoch `saturating_add(1)` at `u64::MAX` is a no-op
rotate.rs:1128: at `current_read_epoch == u64::MAX` the new epoch equals the current one (collision, no advance). Unreachable at 2^64 rotations; noting for completeness only.

## Strongest residual risk
**F1** — the read-revoke guarantee is only partial until the eager-set descendant cascade (#745/#746) is wired. This slice's primitives are individually correct; the danger is treating a root-only cut as a completed revocation. **F2** is the most valuable cheap hardening to fold in now, because the same shared helper will fan out across differently-pseudonymed descendants in exactly that cascade.

No code changes made — review only. Relevant files: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/rotation/{reseal.rs,rotate.rs,trigger.rs,mod.rs}`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/grants/ledger.rs`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/core/src/seal/{grant.rs,section.rs}`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/seams/floor_store.rs`.</result>

## F2 — release-active signer-binding guard

New error variant in `ResealError` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs`):
```rust
/// The rotator's pseudonym signer is not the owner-committed pseudonym key.
/// ... enforced here so a re-seal never signs a section the gate would refuse
/// (fail-closed symmetry).
SignerNotCommitted,
```
Display: `"rotator signer is not the owner-committed pseudonym key"` · `check()`: `"signer-not-committed"` (no key material).

Guard at the top of `reseal_scope_root`, before any sealing:
```rust
if identity.pseudonym_signer.verifying_key().to_bytes()
    != committed.commitment.owner_pseudonym_pk
{
    return Err(ResealError::SignerNotCommitted);
}
```
Plain byte compare (pseudonym pubkey is public). Test: `signer_not_committed_fails_closed_release_active` — plain `#[test]`, wrong signer, asserts `check() == "signer-not-committed"`, no secret bytes.

## CRYPTO/TRUST VERIFICATION — PR #747, `revoke_read_grant` owner-authorization binding

Scope: `crates/engine/src/rotation/trigger.rs` (new file, head `2616314ef`), compared against the adoption gate `crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs` Stage 2 and the core preimage in `crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs`.

The fix under review (`trigger.rs:1808-1811` in the diff):
```rust
let current_sig = EcdsaSignature::from_compact(commitment_sig).ok_or(RevokeError::UnauthorizedSigner)?;
verify_grant_set(&amp;owner_signer.verifying_key(), commitment, &amp;current_sig)
    .map_err(|_| RevokeError::UnauthorizedSigner)?;
```

### 1. PREIMAGE COMPLETENESS — CONFIRMED SOUND
The encode-side check calls the exact same function the gate calls: `verify_grant_set` (`crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs:766`). That function encodes the **full** commitment via `encode_grant_set_commitment` (grant.rs:735) — `entries` + `ipnsName` + `ownerPseudonymPk` in canonical det-CBOR — and verifies RFC-6979 ECDSA over it.

The adoption gate Stage 2 (`adoption.rs`, the `verify_grant_set(reader.owner_identity, &amp;section.commitment, &amp;commitment_sig)` call) verifies the identical preimage, plus a separate `commitment.ipns_name == candidate.name` binding. There is **no narrowing**: the encode-side check authenticates the same fields, same order, same domain separation as the gate. An attacker cannot present a signature valid over a narrower preimage — the whole commitment (including `ipnsName`, which scope-binds it, and `ownerPseudonymPk`, which writer-binds it) is inside the signed bytes. The `ipns_name`/`owner_pseudonym_pk` are preserved verbatim through the cut (only `entries` are pruned; confirmed by `revoke_preserves_survivors_and_owner_fields`), so the re-signed commitment keeps the gate-bound name.

### 2. CORRECT KEY — CONFIRMED SOUND (not circular in the real trust model)
The concern is that `owner_signer` is a caller-supplied signer, so the check could be self-consistent-but-meaningless. It is not, because of the direction of trust:

- `owner_signer` is the **trust anchor**, not the thing being authenticated. In this design it is the local user's Web3Auth-derived identity signer — a secret-key holder, and the operation is entirely client-side (the user *is* the owner; the untrusted server never calls this).
- The check uses that trusted secret key to **authenticate the caller-supplied `commitment`/`commitment_sig`**. To pass, `commitment_sig` must verify under `owner_signer.verifying_key()`. Forging such a signature requires the owner's private key, which an attacker does not have. So a tampered/foreign commitment is rejected (`UnauthorizedSigner`).
- Transitive anchoring to the gate: the current `commitment_sig` is one the gate already verified against `reader.owner_identity` (the contact-anchored identity) at resolve. Requiring `owner_signer` to verify that same sig binds `owner_signer.verifying_key()` to `reader.owner_identity`. The comment at trigger.rs:1806-1807 is accurate.

ECDSA two-candidate-key subtlety (a given message+sig can verify under up to 2 public keys): even if an attacker supplied an `owner_signer` whose key is the *other* recoverable key, (a) they'd need that key's **private** scalar to produce the fresh re-sign — recovery yields only a public key, and (b) the gate re-verifies the re-signed commitment against `reader.owner_identity`, so any re-sign under a non-owner key is **rejected at resolve** regardless. The gate remains the final arbiter; this encode-side check is a fail-fast mirror that prevents minting an unopenable root.

### 3. ORDERING — CONFIRMED SOUND
The signature verify (trigger.rs:1808-1811) runs **before** the `NotGranted` check and **before** any mutation. The mutation operates on `commitment.clone()` + a filtered copy of `grant_ledger` (inputs are `&amp;`-borrowed, never mutated in place). A failed verify, a `NotGranted`, or a `Sign` error returns with zero partial state — no partially-pruned committed set is possible.

### 4. FAIL-CLOSED — CONFIRMED SOUND
`RevokeError::UnauthorizedSigner` is a real runtime `Err` variant returned via `.ok_or(...)` / `.map_err(...)` — no `debug_assert!`/`assert!`, so it is release-active. The error carries no secret material: `check()` returns static strings; `Display` writes static text (and only a `CodecError.check()` classifier on the `Sign` variant). Malformed-signature bytes also fail closed via `from_compact(...).ok_or(UnauthorizedSigner)`.

### 5. TEST ADEQUACY — CONFIRMED with a minor gap (not a defect)
`revoke_wrong_signer_fails_closed` genuinely exercises the bypass: it passes a `wrong_owner` (scalar `0x44`) against a `commitment_sig` produced by the real owner (`0x33`), and asserts `unauthorized-signer`. That covers **key-identity** rejection.

Gap: there is **no engine-level test** for the *right* signer paired with a `commitment_sig` over a *different/tampered* commitment (the preimage-binding case) — nor for the malformed-`from_compact` path. This is a coverage gap, not a soundness hole: the preimage binding is enforced by `verify_grant_set`, which **is** tamper-tested at the core layer (`grant.rs:944-948`, "tampered commitment rejects"). Recommend (non-blocking) adding one engine test: real `owner_signer`, but a `commitment_sig` signed over a mutated commitment → expect `unauthorized-signer`, to lock the binding at this layer.

### Strongest residual risk
The check's soundness rests on the caller contract that `owner_signer` is the genuine session owner identity and `commitment`/`commitment_sig` is the authentic gate-verified current pair. In this slice `revoke_read_grant` has **no production caller** (only tests; resolver/tree wiring lands in #745/#746). When the real caller is wired, it must feed the gate-resolved `(commitment, commitment_sig)` and the session owner signer — not any network-derived unverified record. Even if that contract were violated, the adoption gate's `verify_grant_set(reader.owner_identity, ...)` at resolve is the fail-closed backstop, so an unauthorized re-sign cannot be *adopted*. Worth a tracked note on the #745/#746 wiring to pass gate-verified inputs.

### Bottom line: CONFIRMED-SOUND
The `commitmentSig` verification reconstructs exactly the gate's preimage (same `verify_grant_set` / `encode_grant_set_commitment` det-CBOR bytes), anchors the re-sign to the owner identity via the trusted secret signer, runs before any mutation, and fails closed as a release-active `Err` with no secret leakage. An unauthorized party cannot get a revocation/re-sign **adopted**: a non-owner re-sign is rejected here at encode time and, as final backstop, by the gate's `reader.owner_identity` verify at resolve. No preimage mismatch between this encode-side check and the gate's decode-side check exists. The only follow-ups are a non-blocking preimage-binding test at the engine layer and a wiring note for the future caller to pass gate-verified inputs.

- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs` (Stage 2 decode-side mirror)
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs:735-776` (shared preimage + verify)</result>

## #749 verdict: READY-NOW-IN-SIMULATION

The owner-revocation eager cascade is buildable now against injected/faked seams, exactly as Slices 2/3 were. It is **not** hard-blocked on #745/#746. The #749 body itself states this ("Simulation-testable earlier against the injected `ChildIndexResolver` seam + faked seams, exactly as #744/#747"), and the code confirms every per-descendant input is either a pure in-walk computation or an already-modeled impure seam.

Per-item evidence (the four inputs a descendant re-key needs):

**Item 3 — parent's new derivation (ascent link): PURE, computed in-walk. Not blocked.**
The child's ascent link seals under a keypair derived from `parent_node_seed` (`crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs:456-462`, `kdf::ascent_keypair`), and `parent_node_seed = kdf::node_seed(parent_override_seed, child_scope_id)` — confirmed by the resolve-side mirror at `crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs:541` (`node_seed = kdf::node_seed(payload.override_seed(), &amp;candidate.envelope.id)`). `kdf::node_seed` (`crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs:229`) and `kdf::ascent_keypair` (`:261`) are pure KDF edges. Because the cascade rotates **parent before child** (top-down), the parent's freshly-minted seed is in-hand when the child is re-keyed — the child's `parent_node_seed` is a local KDF call, needing **no external resolve**.

**Item 4 — child index: already an injected seam. Not blocked.**
`ChildIndexResolver::direct_child_index` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/eager_set.rs:176-179`) returns only `Vec&lt;ChildScopeRef&gt;` — the index and nothing else. It is faked in-slice today (`FakeResolver`, `eager_set.rs:300-312`). It does **not** surface items 1-2.

**Items 1-2 — current seed + committed set: obtainable via a faked sibling seam in simulation; require the real resolver only in production.**
- The `OwnerSeedCache` (`crates/engine/src/grants/owner_entry.rs:44-71`) is the canonical owner-session source of a descendant's current override seed, but it holds `{seed, epoch}` **only for scopes already confirmed-read this session** — `get` returns `Option` (`:56`), and "cold start adopts nothing until the first confirmed read" (`:51`). So in **production** it does not hold the whole eager set at rotation time; obtaining an unread descendant's current seed + committed set genuinely requires a live resolve→gate→unseal of that descendant's record (owner blob → seed; write-body → ledger + child index) — which is the #745/#746 wiring.
- In **simulation**, that resolve/gate/unseal edge is an impure seam, identical in shape to `ChildIndexResolver` and `ScopeRootPublisher`. The cascade injects a per-descendant reseal-inputs seam (yielding `{current override_seed, current read_epoch}`, `{commitment, commitment_sig, grant_ledger}`, carried history links, write scope seed/epoch, pointer_read_key) and the test fakes it. The pure orchestration — top-down walk, mint fresh seed per node from the entropy seam, derive each child's `parent_node_seed` via `kdf::node_seed`, re-key via `reseal_scope_root` with `prev = Some`, publish CAS, fail-closed on any descendant that can't be rotated — is fully buildable and testable now.

**Reseal primitive already accepts everything a descendant re-key needs** — confirmed. `reseal_scope_root` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs:201-207`) takes `ScopeRootIdentity{ parent_node_seed: Some(...), pseudonym_signer, ... }`, `ResealSeeds{ override_seed(fresh), read_epoch, prev: Some(current seed+epoch), write_scope_seed, write_epoch, pointer_read_key }`, `CommittedSet{ commitment, commitment_sig, grant_ledger, write_history_link, direct_child_scope_index }`, and `carried_history_links`. Nothing is missing for the descendant case. The `revoked_grantee_is_absent...` test (`reseal.rs:684-792`) already proves fresh-seed re-key with `prev` on the exact revocation property.

**#745/#746 do NOT gate simulation.** #745 enforces *production* `scope_id → gated-record` binding in the real resolver; #746 decides the *production* same-`scope_id`/different-`ipns_name` conflict. Both are resolver-binding **correctness** decisions with "no live rotation path today" (#746 body). A faked resolver in simulation needs neither.

**One real in-slice design note (not a blocker):** `rotate_scope` (`rotation/rotate.rs:196-276`) mints its seed internally as `Zeroizing` (`:222`) and returns only `RotationOutcome{ new_read_epoch, epoch_floor }` (`:178`) — it does **not** surface the minted seed. So the cascade cannot reuse `rotate_scope` as-is to thread each parent's new seed into children's ascent links. It must mint seeds at the orchestrator (entropy seam) and drive `reseal_scope_root` directly (which takes `override_seed` as a parameter) + publish, or extend `rotate_scope` to return the seed. Also, `enumerate_eager_set` returns descendants flat, `scope_id`-ascending (`eager_set.rs:237-239`) — not parent-first — so the cascade needs the tree **edges** (parent→child) for seed threading, i.e. it walks top-down itself using the resolver's per-node index rather than consuming the flat `EagerSet` list. `EagerSet` gives the completeness proof; the cascade needs the linkage.

**Seams to inject for the simulation slice:** (a) the existing `ChildIndexResolver` (#744, built); (b) a new per-descendant reseal-inputs resolver (current seed/epoch + committed set + sig + carried history links + write material); (c) the existing `ScopeRootPublisher`; (d) `Entropy`, `FloorStore`, `Scheduler`. All fakeable.

## #745 / #746 verdict: buildable, but production-gated; ~one slice each (or one combined), best done together

- **Entails:** replacing the faked `ChildIndexResolver` (and the #749 reseal-inputs seam) with a real edge that resolves a descendant's record over the real `RecordTransport`, runs the adoption gate, unseals the write-body, and derives each descendant's `scope_id` **solely from the gated parent index entry** (#745), plus deciding the same-`scope_id`/different-`ipns_name` conflict (hard-abort vs. one-authoritative-name, #746).
- **#745 = enforcement** (resolver must never let a network source influence `scope_id` independently of the gated parent record + a binding test: a gate-passing-but-mislabeled entry must be rejected). **#746 = the conflict decision** it depends on. They are two tightly-coupled decisions on the same code edge — practically one resolver-wiring slice, or #746's decision folded into #745's enforcement. Neither is buildable to *completion* without the live resolve path, which overlaps heavily with #752's live-session resolve wiring.
- **Unblocks (production):** production sweep resolution, the **production** #749 cascade (real per-descendant seed/committed-set), and real write-plane resolution. Not needed for the #749 *simulation* slice.

## #752 verdict: large umbrella (multi-slice), separate track, upstream-unblocked

- **Scope:** the full facade cold-start — Web3Auth/challenge-signature login → session key material (per-scope read/write keys, per-name `Ed25519Signer`s, writer-pseudonym signers) → live-session wiring (the resolve path, held-record set, liveness-loop composition, rehydrate-on-boot) — behind the frozen `Engine::start` surface.
- **Size:** umbrella, not one slice; it explicitly **blocks #750** (compose `eol_republish` seq+1 renewal into the liveness loop) and **#751** (populate the held-record set from the live resolve path).
- **Deps:** #624 (identity auth + refresh) and #623 (facade scaffold) — both **merged**. No blockers.
- **Unblocks:** #750, #751, and downstream the web shell (#642/#643) and desktop login (#650). This is the track that assembles the **real** resolve path that #745/#746 and the production #749 cascade ultimately consume — a separate concern from the read-plane rotation logic itself.
