## Recommended build order

1. **#749 (simulation cascade)** — build now, next, on top of merged #744/#747 + Slice 3. Zero hard dependency on #745/#746/#752; completes the read-revoke *logic* fail-closed against faked seams. Rationale: it's the highest-value correctness gap (the actual read-revoke completion) and is unblocked today.
2. **#752 (facade cold-start)** — the live-session/resolve-path track; large, independently unblocked (auth merged). Assembles the real resolve edge everything else needs.
3. **#745 + #746 (resolver binding + conflict, together)** — layer onto the live resolve path from #752; turns the faked resolver into a gated, bound production edge. Rationale: they *are* the production correctness of the edge #752 builds and #749 consumes, so they must land **with/after** the live resolve path, not before.
4. **Production #749 wiring** — swap #749's faked reseal-inputs seam for the real resolver from #745/#746 + #752; this is what makes the cascade production-real.

**Hard dependencies:** #745/#746 → the live resolve path (#752). Production #749 → #745/#746 + #752. The #749 *simulation* slice depends on none of them (only #744/#747, merged, + Slice 3 sweep).

## Single recommended NEXT slice once Slice 3 (sweep) merges: **#749 — the owner-revocation eager cascade, in simulation.**

Why: it is the one deferral that is fully unblocked today, it is the missing read-revoke completion (the security-critical gap Slice 3 explicitly does *not* provide), and it reuses the already-built primitives (`enumerate_eager_set` #744, `reseal_scope_root` with `prev=Some` #747) plus a fakeable per-descendant reseal-inputs seam — exactly the Slice-2/3 pattern. #745/#746/#752 are larger, mutually entangled with the live resolve path, and gate only the *production* wiring, which should follow the facade track — not block the cascade logic from landing and being tested now.</result>

## Summary

**PR:** #753 (DRAFT) — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/753

### Spec ambiguity — resolved to Option A (as you confirmed)
The sweep is **metadata-only epoch-lag convergence** with the **existing** seed (`prev=None`), not the fresh-seed descendant re-key. That fresh-seed eager cascade is `rotateScope`'s job, deferred to #745/#746. I corrected `trigger.rs`'s doc to attribute the descendant re-key to `rotateScope`, dropped the "no cached seed survives" overclaim, and assert completeness as **epoch-convergence**, not seed-freshness.

### What landed
- **Public entry points:** `sweep_pass` (one idempotent pass) and `run_sweep` (idle-cadence driver), in new `crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep.rs`.
- **Reuse of `reseal_scope_root`:** for each lagging descendant it builds `ResealSeeds { override_seed: &lt;existing&gt;, read_epoch: &lt;descendant's own floor&gt;, prev: None, write_epoch: &lt;unchanged&gt; }` and calls `reseal_scope_root` — no fresh seed, no epoch bump, no new history link.
- **Completeness fail-closed semantics:** work-list is the epoch-lag predicate over published records only (no pending-store). A node that can't be enumerated/resolved/re-sealed/published is never silently skipped — the pass hard-aborts with a `SweepError` naming the scope (mirrors the eager-set walk, `engine.md` L269-274). Trust failures are fatal; availability failures retryable. The one non-abort is a lost CAS race (`engine.md` L276-278).
- **Concurrency/idempotency:** sweep raises no floor (no double-advance); CAS loser drops cleanly and re-resolves. Key finding folded from security review: since the CAS is keyed on IPNS *sequence*, a lost race can be to a non-advancing writer, so `run_sweep` now loops until a pass drops nothing (bounded), surfacing residuals rather than returning a false "complete."
- **Self-heal:** `canonicalize` + flag the direct-child-scope index on each re-seal (`SweepOutcome.flagged_indexes`).

# Crypto/Trust Review — PR #753 (the sweep, #635 Slice 3)

Scope: concurrency correctness, fail-closed completeness, termination. Crypto path (`reseal_scope_root`, `prev=None`) taken as previously reviewed on #747. Read-only; no edits.

## Bottom line

The sweep is sound on all three failure axes: it cannot (a) silently leave a lagging node, (b) infinite-loop or false-complete on adversarial CAS races, or (c) double-advance/clobber under concurrent sweepers. Findings below are CONFIRMED per checklist point, with one load-bearing cross-slice invariant flagged as the strongest residual risk.

## Checklist verdicts

**1. Fail-closed completeness — CONFIRMED, no silent drop.** Every descendant in `sweep_pass` (crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep.rs:366-463) lands in exactly one terminal disposition or aborts the whole pass naming a scope. Traced: `cache.remove().expect(...)` (panics, never silent — and the 1:1 cache/eager-set invariant genuinely holds: `enumerate_eager_set` inserts into `discovered` then resolves, and only successfully-resolved nodes reach `descendants()`); floor read `Err` → `SweepError::Floor` (abort); `None`/at-or-above floor → `already_converged`; reseal `Err` → `SweepError::Reseal` (abort); publish `Ok`→`converged`, `LostRace`→`dropped_lost_race`, `NotPublished`→`SweepError::Publish` (abort). Both `continue` early-exits push to `already_converged` first — no lagging node is dropped without a bucket or a hard abort. The trust-fatal vs availability-retryable split in `is_retryable` (sweep.rs:292-299) is correct: Enumeration `Rejected`/Reseal fatal; Enumeration `Unavailable`/Floor/Publish retryable.

**2. Lost-CAS-race convergence — CONFIRMED, the folded finding is correctly handled.**
- (a) Termination bound is `max_passes`: every `loop` iteration increments `attempts`, and both continuing arms are gated (`attempts &gt;= max_passes` returns in the Ok arm; `attempts &lt; max_passes` guards the retryable-Err arm; a non-retryable Err returns immediately). An adversarial steady non-advancing writer cannot loop forever — it is capped. No `attempts` overflow (exits at `&gt;= max_passes`).
- (b) No false-complete: `run_sweep` returns `Ok` immediately only when `dropped_lost_race.is_empty()` (sweep.rs:521). A `LostRace` is routed to `dropped_lost_race`, never `converged`, so a non-advancing winner never counts as converged.
- (c) On cap exhaustion the residual is surfaced in `SweepOutcome::dropped_lost_race` (sweep.rs:524-527), distinguishable from true convergence (`converged`/`already_converged` populated, `dropped_lost_race` empty). The non-advancing-writer test (`run_sweep_loops_past_non_advancing_lost_race_until_it_wins`) and the cap test (`run_sweep_surfaces_residual_drop_on_cap_exhaustion`) genuinely exercise the epoch-not-moving publisher arm (`lost_race_next`), so these are non-tautological. Cap soundness is fine; see the minor residual on caller inspection below.

**3. Idempotency / no double-advance — CONFIRMED.** The sweep raises no floor — production code only ever calls `floors.epoch_floor(&amp;scope_id)` (read); `raise_epoch_floor` appears solely in test setup. Re-running once converged is a no-op (`already_converged`; `already_converged_nodes_are_not_resealed` asserts zero re-publish). Two sweepers reseal the same node at the same floor with the same seed; the sequence-keyed CAS lets one land and the other take `LostRace` → drop → re-resolve as converged. No floor double-advance (no floor write at all), no clobber (CAS).

**4. Metadata-only invariant — CONFIRMED.** Reseal is called with `prev: None`, `override_seed` = existing seed, `read_epoch: floor_epoch`, `write_epoch` unchanged (sweep.rs:413-421). `reseal_scope_root` mints a history link only under `Some(prev)` (reseal.rs:315), so none is appended. Descendant is re-sealed to **its own** per-scope floor epoch (`floors.epoch_floor(&amp;scope_id)`), which is correct per the floor law — each scope root's epoch is independent, not the root's cut epoch. `metadata_only_reuses_seed_no_epoch_bump_no_history_link` proves the owner blob still decrypts to the existing seed at exactly the floor epoch with empty `history_links`.

**5. Determinism — CONFIRMED.** Cadence enters only via `Scheduler::sleep`; the pass reads no clock and samples no RNG beyond the injected `Entropy` handed to reseal. Work-list order is `eager_set.descendants()` = BTreeMap ascending `scope_id` (deterministic, permutation-independent). `determinism_same_entropy_same_published_bytes` confirms byte-stable publishes.

**6. Doc correctness — CONFIRMED.** The trigger.rs and mod.rs doc corrections accurately state that fresh-seed descendant re-key is `rotateScope`'s eager-set republish (deferred #745/#746), not the sweep's; and that the sweep "does not on its own complete a read revoke" and "reuses the existing seed and never re-keys." No residual overclaim that the sweep revokes a cached seed.

**7. Test adequacy — CONFIRMED, interleavings are real not tautological.** The two lost-race publisher arms are distinct and meaningful: `fault(LostRace)` = concurrent *sweeper* winner that advances the epoch (drop → converges); `lost_race_next` = *non-advancing* ordinary writer (drop → still lagging, drives the loop). `partial_sweep_then_resume_converges_all` proves a mid-pass `NotPublished` abort leaves the already-published node durably converged and the resume finishes the rest. Minor gaps (not blocking): (i) a node that *re-lags after being converged* within one driver run isn't directly tested — but it's symmetric to a fresh pass; (ii) the "writer advances between enumerate and reseal" interleaving is modeled via injected `LostRace` rather than an organic sequence bump against a concurrently-mutated record — acceptable, since the real sequence-CAS keying is the publisher seam's contract (#745/#746), and the sweep's response (drop + re-resolve) is what's under test here.

**8. Preimage-binding test — CONFIRMED genuine.** `revoke_tampered_commitment_preimage_fails_closed` signs with the *correct owner key* over a *mutated* commitment (`tampered.ipns_name`), then revokes the *original* commitment presenting that tampered-preimage signature. The `assert_ne!` confirms it differs from the authentic signature, so it is a true preimage-tamper case (right signer, wrong message) distinct from the wrong-signer case, and it lands on `UnauthorizedSigner`.

## Strongest residual risk (cross-slice invariant to protect)

The sweep advances a descendant's published record epoch to its floor **using the stale existing seed**. This is safe *only because* revocation re-keys descendants via the (still-deferred) eager cascade with a fresh seed at a fresh epoch — **not** by raising descendant epoch floors and leaning on the sweep. If the #745/#746 wiring ever makes a revocation raise *descendant* epoch floors expecting the sweep to enforce it, the sweep would advance the record epoch with the old seed, making the record read "converged" (`record_epoch &gt;= floor`) while the revokee's cached seed still opens it — a masked incomplete revoke. The doc corrections in this PR guard the boundary correctly today; this is the load-bearing assumption to hold when the deferred eager cascade lands. Worth an explicit note/test in #745/#746 that revocation-driven descendant floor raises must be paired with a fresh-seed re-key, never left to the metadata-only sweep.

## Minor (non-blocking)

- `run_sweep` returns `Ok(outcome)` on cap exhaustion with the residual in `dropped_lost_race`; a caller checking only `result.is_ok()` would read it as complete. The contract requires inspecting the outcome buckets. Documented in the `run_sweep` rustdoc — fine, but a host-side lint/assert that `dropped_lost_race.is_empty()` on a "done" verdict would harden it.
- `sweep_pass` is `pub` and also returns `Ok` with populated `dropped_lost_race`; only `run_sweep` loops to true convergence. Same caller-inspection caveat; the intended entry point is `run_sweep`.

Implemented **#749** — the `rotateScope` owner-revocation eager cascade — as a DRAFT PR. Left it draft; not flipped to ready or merged.

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/755 (draft) · head sha `54a9bec721d2c55a16a447d6e7a61c1b3d213a15` · branch `feat/engine-eager-cascade`

All local gates green: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, plus a `--release` run confirming the fail-closed guards fire in release.

**Files:**
- `crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs` (new) — `cascade_rotate_scope` + the `CascadeResealResolver`/`CascadeTarget` seam, `CascadeError`/`CascadeOutcome`/`RekeyedScope`, and 12 simulation tests (all pass).
- `crates/engine/src/rotation/mod.rs` — registered/exported the module; refreshed the rotation-module doc.
- `crates/engine/src/rotation/trigger.rs` — read-revoke doc now states the cascade (not the sweep) completes the revoke (issue AC).

**Design decisions worth your eye:**
1. **The cascade re-keys the root itself** (does not delegate the root to `rotate_scope`). The seed-threading requirement forces the orchestrator to hold the root's fresh seed, and `rotate_scope` doesn't surface its minted seed — so `cascade_rotate_scope` mints every fresh seed (root + descendants) through the injected `Entropy` seam via a shared private `rekey_one` helper (reuses `reseal_scope_root` with `prev = Some`). `rotate_scope` stays as-is for the flat scope-exit/manual triggers.
2. **New seam:** `CascadeResealResolver` returning `CascadeTarget`. I did *not* reuse the sweep's `SweepResolver`/`SweepTarget` — `CascadeTarget` deliberately **omits `parent_node_seed`**, encoding the cascade's core invariant that the parent derivation is always threaded (`node_seed(parent_fresh_seed, child_scope_id)`), never taken from the descendant's stale record. That semantic distinction (the sweep *does* carry it, because it reuses the published derivation) justified a dedicated type over reuse. Its fake in tests is `FakeNet`, a shared-map resolver+publisher that records published records so the tests recover fresh seeds via owner blobs and probe ascent links.

**Cross-slice invariant assertion:** `revocation_is_a_fresh_seed_cascade_not_an_epoch_bump` — after the cascade a descendant's published override seed **changes** (a floor-raise + sweep would leave it *equal*, since the sweep uses `prev = None`), and exactly one fresh history link is ratcheted (a sweep appends none). Backed by `old_descendant_seed_holder_is_locked_out` (cached old seed no longer derives the current read key) and `parent_child_seed_threading_is_correct` (the child's ascent link opens only under the parent's *new* derivation, not the stale one).

**Deferred to #745/#746 (confirm the issue links):** the production resolver/tree wiring of the `CascadeResealResolver` edge — the live resolve/adoption-gate/unseal path and the `scope_id`-binding/conflict-resolution enforcement. This slice fakes that seam and proves the cascade in simulation (exactly as #744/#747 did). Because that production wiring is a dependency and out of this slice's scope, I used **"Part of #749"** in the PR body rather than `Closes` — the cascade logic + simulation proof is complete, but I left the closure call to you (and #745/#746 stand as the wiring follow-ups). If you consider #749's scope satisfied by the simulation deliverable, switch it to `Closes #749`.

No spec contradictions hit — engine.md L243-252 was authoritative and the model held together.</result>

# Security Review — PR #755 `feat/engine-eager-cascade` (owner-revocation eager cascade)

**Scope reviewed:** `git diff main...feat/engine-eager-cascade` — `crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs` (new, 1205 lines), `mod.rs` (+export/doc), `trigger.rs` (doc only). `reseal.rs`/`rotate.rs` are unchanged deps and out of the newly-introduced surface.

**Verdict: PASS — no HIGH or MEDIUM security findings.** The cascade is correctly fail-closed, mints fresh distinct seeds through the `Entropy` seam, threads parent→child derivations correctly, is iterative with cycle/diamond termination, and its epoch-exhaustion guard is a release-active `Err` with no `debug_assert`. One LOW/informational latent binding ambiguity handed to the resolver-wiring slices as issue #756. No code folded (no behavior change warranted in this slice).

## Domain-focus checklist results

- **Fail-closed completeness — PASS.** The non-test code has exactly two `Ok(...)` returns (rekey_one, cascade_rotate_scope), both reachable only after `?`-propagating every resolve/reseal/publish/floor/entropy/epoch failure as a scope-naming `CascadeError`. A visited-set `continue` only skips an already-re-keyed scope. There is no path that returns `Ok` with an unprocessed reachable descendant. Partial durable re-keys before an abort are monotonic (fresh seed at higher epoch = only more lockout) and success is never *claimed* — the sweep enqueue is the last step, skipped on any abort (verified by `spawned == 0` in every abort test).
- **Fresh-seed cascade, not floor+sweep — PASS.** Each `rekey_one` calls `entropy.fill(fresh_seed)` (`prev = Some(current seed)`), so every scope gets a distinct fresh seed. Asserted by `every_descendant_gets_a_distinct_fresh_seed_and_root_first` (pairwise-distinct, differs from pre-cascade) and `revocation_is_a_fresh_seed_cascade_not_an_epoch_bump` (published seed changes + one history link ratcheted, vs a sweep's zero).
- **Top-down threading — PASS.** Child ascent link re-seals under `node_seed(parent_fresh_seed, child.scope_id)`; `CascadeTarget` deliberately omits any stale `parent_node_seed`. `parent_child_seed_threading_is_correct` proves B's ascent opens **only** under A's *new* derivation, not the stale one — the ancestor-path lockout.
- **Determinism — PASS.** Entropy only via the `Entropy` seam, time only via `Scheduler`; the walk reads no clock and samples no RNG. `canonicalize`/`canonicalize_frontier` give a permutation-independent walk order (`re_run_is_deterministic_and_locks_out_each_time`).
- **Encode/decode fail-closed symmetry — PASS.** The one encode-side guard added here (epoch bump) is `checked_add(1).ok_or(EpochExhausted)` — a runtime `Err`, no `debug_assert!`/`assert!` anywhere in non-test code (grep-confirmed). `epoch_exhausted_descendant_aborts_release_active` exercises it and asserts nothing published. The guards are structurally release-active (no debug-only construct exists to strip).
- **DoS / unbounded work — PASS (and out of exclusion scope anyway).** The walk is iterative (explicit frontier, no recursion → no stack blowup) with a `scope_id`-keyed visited set; cycles (`cycle_back_edge_terminates`) and diamonds (`diamond_descendant_rekeyed_once_under_first_seen_parent`) terminate at O(distinct descendant count).

## LOW / Informational — filed as follow-up, not a finding against this slice

**`cascade.rs` — `scope_id` authority doc/code mismatch (latent binding gap for #745/#746).** `cascade_rotate_scope` derives the child parent node seed and visited-set key from the **enumerated** `child.scope_id`, but sets `RotateScopePlan.identity.scope_id = target.scope_id` and publishes/floor-raises under the **resolver-returned** `target.scope_id`. The `CascadeResealResolver` trait doc claims the engine "re-seals and publishes under the enumerated parent-index `scope_id` (never a resolver-returned one)" — which the code does not do. Not exploitable in #755 (resolver is faked; fake always returns equal ids), but if the production resolver (#745/#746) does not enforce `target.scope_id == child.scope_id`, a divergent resolve would cross-wire the publish destination vs. the threaded ascent derivation — a revocation-hole class bug that a reviewer trusting the doc would not expect the resolver to have to defend. Recommended fix in #745/#746: make the enumerated id authoritative (use `child.scope_id` for identity/publish/floor, drop or demote `target.scope_id`), or bind it in the resolver and correct the doc. **Filed: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/756** (label `v2-build`, refs #749/#755, binds #745/#746).

## CRYPTO-PRIVACY REVIEW COMPLETE — PR #755 `feat/engine-eager-cascade`

**Files analyzed:** 3 (`crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs` new +1205, `mod.rs`, `trigger.rs`)
**Crypto operations found:** fresh-seed minting (Entropy seam), top-down parent→child seed threading (`kdf::node_seed`), per-scope re-seal via `reseal_scope_root` (`prev = Some`), history-link ratchet, CAS publish + floor raise.
**Verdict: PASS — no blocking crypto findings. No fold commit. Do not weaken any guard.**

I verified every invariant in the brief against the actual core primitives it composes (`crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs`, `crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs`, `crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs`, `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs`), not just the diff's prose. All 12 simulation tests pass in `--release`, including the epoch-exhaustion guard.

### Invariant-by-invariant confirmation

1. **All crypto in core; engine only composes.** Confirmed — the cascade calls only `kdf::node_seed`, `reseal_scope_root`, and the seams. No ad-hoc crypto.
2. **Override seeds RANDOM, never KDF-derived; each descendant DISTINCT.** `rekey_one` mints via `entropy.fill()` (`cascade.rs:365-371`) — never through the KDF. Each `rekey_one` call draws a fresh seed from the injected Entropy stream; `every_descendant_gets_a_distinct_fresh_seed_and_root_first` proves distinctness with `ct_eq_32`.
3. **Seed threading is top-down from the parent's NEW seed; `CascadeTarget` omits `parent_node_seed`.** Confirmed the load-bearing point: `child_parent_node_seed = node_seed(parent_fresh_seed, child.scope_id)` (`cascade.rs:516-517`), and `CascadeTarget` structurally carries no parent seed (`cascade.rs:114-151`) so a stale-derivation is impossible to construct. This matches CONTEXT.md L14 ("keypair derived from the parent's node seed, never the child's own") and the adoption gate's open key at `adoption.rs:539` — same `node_seed(parent_override, child_id)` value on both seal and open sides. `parent_child_seed_threading_is_correct` proves B's ascent opens under `node_seed(A_fresh, B)` and **not** under `node_seed(A_old, B)` — i.e., a revoked ancestor holding A's stale derivation cannot reach B.
4. **Zeroization at terminal owner only.** All seed material is `Zeroizing`; seeds pass to `reseal_scope_root` by borrow (`&amp;fresh_seed`, `cascade.rs:374`), and the callee never zeroes caller buffers (verified in `reseal.rs`). Frontier-held `parent_seed` clones are `Zeroizing`, wiped when the frontier is reassigned; `root_fresh_seed` is explicitly `drop`ped after seeding level-1 (`cascade.rs:491`).
5. **No secret in log/assert/format.** `CascadeError::Display` and `check()` emit only `hex_lower(scope_id)` (public 16-byte node id) and a stable class string. Tests use `ct_eq_32`, never `assert_eq!` on seed bytes.
6. **Fail-closed at the trust boundary.** Any resolve/reseal/publish/floor failure hard-aborts naming the scope (`CascadeError`), returning `Err` instead of a `CascadeOutcome` — no partial revoke can be mistaken for complete. LostRace and NotPublished both abort (unlike the idempotent sweep). Sweep is enqueued only after the whole cascade is durable, so an aborted run enqueues nothing (proven: `spawned == 0` on every abort path). Epoch exhaustion is a release-active `Err` (`checked_add`, `cascade.rs:358-361`), never `debug_assert!` — per AGENTS.md rule 8, and it fires in the release test run I executed.
7. **`reseal_scope_root(prev = Some(fresh_seed))` + ratchet.** Correct: the fresh seed is the new override seed at `epoch+1`; `prev` seals the OLD seed under the NEW epoch's structure key → exactly one fresh history link per rotated scope (`revocation_is_a_fresh_seed_cascade_not_an_epoch_bump` asserts `history_len == 1`; a sweep would append none). History links point new→old, so a revoked holder of an old seed gains nothing forward.

### Concrete failure scenario each guard defeats
- **Cached-descendant-seed revocation hole:** a reader who cached descendant D's override seed. Floor-raise + sweep (`prev = None`) would walk that cached seed forward; the cascade mints a fresh D seed so `read_key(node_seed(old, D))` no longer matches the current record (`old_descendant_seed_holder_is_locked_out`).
- **Stale ascent path for a revoked ancestor:** without top-down threading, D's ascent would still open under `node_seed(A_stale, D)`, leaving a revoked A a path in. The thread from A's fresh seed closes it.
- **Silent partial revoke:** an unresolvable/unpublished/lost-race descendant returning "complete" would leave a live seed. Hard-abort prevents it.

### Non-blocking observations (no fix required, no issue filed)
- **Diamond first-seen-parent threading:** a multi-parent descendant D is re-keyed once, threaded from the canonically-first parent (A over B). Descending via the *other* parent B cannot open D's ascent link. This is not a revocation hole — D is re-keyed with a fresh seed regardless of thread parent, and the owner retains the owner-blob path at every scope root (CONTEXT.md L15). It mirrors `enumerate_eager_set`'s existing first-seen discipline and the transient dual-link reconciliation (CONTEXT.md L55). Inherent to the single-ascent-link data model, not introduced here.
- **Seam still faked:** the per-descendant resolve/gate/publish edge (`CascadeResealResolver`) is simulation-only; the binding contract in its doc (returned `ipns_name` must equal the gated name / `commitment.ipns_name`, never a network hint) is an obligation on the production resolver wiring (#745/#746). That is correctly out of scope for this slice and already tracked. Worth re-auditing that binding surface upfront when #745/#746 land, per the MEMORY.md #682 discipline.

Relevant file: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs`.

No commit made, no issue filed, PR left as-is (not flipped to ready, not merged).</result>

## /simplify on PR #755 — report

Ran the four cleanup-review agents (reuse, simplification, efficiency, altitude) over the new `crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs`, deduped findings, and applied the ones that were behavior-preserving and in-scope.

### Verdicts on the three pressure-test items

1. **`CascadeResealResolver`/`CascadeTarget` vs reusing the sweep's seam — KEEP the separate type.** The `parent_node_seed` omission is a genuine make-illegal-states-unrepresentable guard, not incidental duplication. If `CascadeTarget` carried the field, the single most dangerous maintenance slip — wiring `target.parent_node_seed` (the descendant's *stale published* derivation) into `ScopeRootIdentity` instead of the top-down-threaded `child_parent_node_seed` — becomes representable, and that is precisely the revocation hole the cascade exists to close. A shared type would at best demote it to an always-`None` `Option` (weaker). Unifying would also have to touch out-of-diff `sweep.rs`. The ~13 duplicated field decls are the honest cost of a compile-time guarantee on a security-critical invariant. The only non-churn option — factor the shared owned fields into a `Core` struct with `SweepTarget = Core + parent_node_seed`, `CascadeTarget = Core` — preserves the guard but adds a nesting layer and edits sweep.rs; not warranted here.

2. **`rekey_one` — genuinely shared within the cascade (good), but duplicates `rotate_scope`.** `rekey_one` correctly serves both the root and every descendant through one path — no root/descendant duplication. However it is a near-verbatim copy of `rotate_scope`'s core (epoch-exhaustion guard → mint fresh seed → `ResealSeeds{prev:Some}` → `reseal_scope_root` → build record → publish-then-raise-floor, same crash-safe ordering) in out-of-diff `rotate.rs`. The deeper fix is a lower `rekey_scope_root` primitive beneath both (`rotate_scope` adds the sweep enqueue + drops the seed; `rekey_one` threads the seed + enqueues once), because the cascade legitimately can't just call `rotate_scope` (it enqueues one sweep at the end and needs the fresh seed back). **Deferred, not applied** — it touches a landed, separately-tested file and has differing error/return types (a design change, not a mechanical dedup), i.e. scope-creep for a simplify pass. Recommend filing a follow-up (label `v2-build`) to extract that primitive; I did not auto-file it since it re-architects an out-of-diff file you should own.

3. **Reuse `enumerate_eager_set` — no, irreducibly separate.** `enumerate_eager_set` is enumerate-then-process yielding a flat, edge-less descendant set; the sweep can reuse it because each node processes independently. The cascade is process-while-descending: the value it threads down (a parent's *fresh* override seed) does not exist until that parent is re-keyed, so it cannot be a pure pre-enumeration. Even generalizing the enumerator to carry parent→child edges wouldn't help — the seeds are minted mid-walk, and the fused walk keeps only one node's `Zeroizing` secret material live at a time. The reuse it *does* do (the `visited`-set discipline, `ResolveFailure`, the first-seen-frontier convention) is at the right level.

### Changes applied (commit `f80410ed3`, pushed to `feat/engine-eager-cascade`)

- **Removed double frontier canonicalization (production):** the walk called `canonicalize(...)` on child indexes before handing them to `canonicalize_frontier`, which sorts+dedups by `scope_id` again. `canonicalize_frontier` is the frontier's sole normalization, so the inner calls were redundant work; child indexes now feed in verbatim, and the now-unused import is dropped. Behavior is identical — the `visited` set plus the stable first-seen frontier dedup still thread diamond descendants from their canonically-first parent (the unchanged diamond/determinism/threading tests confirm this).
- **Deduped test fixtures:** hoisted the committed-set construction that `FakeNet::scope` and `RootFx::new` built identically into one `Owner::committed(byte)` helper (~15 lines collapsed, removes a lockstep hazard).

### Skipped
- The `rekey_one`/`rotate_scope` consolidation (item 2) — out-of-diff, scope-creep.
- Stripping `canonicalize_frontier`'s dedup as "redundant with `visited`" (one agent's suggestion) — after removing the inner `canonicalize` calls, that dedup becomes the frontier's *only* normalization and keeps the "first-seen parent wins a diamond" invariant documented in one obvious place; removing it would push a security invariant onto a subtler stable-sort+`visited` interaction for no real gain.

### Local gate state — all green
- `cargo fmt --all --check` ✅
- `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core` ✅ (12/12 cascade tests + full suites)
- `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ✅
- `cargo build -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` ✅

## Report

Doc-only fix landed on PR #755 (`feat/engine-eager-cascade`). No code behavior changed.

**File:** `crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs`

**Before (the false claim in `CascadeResealResolver::resolve` doc):**
&gt; "The cascade re-seals and publishes under the enumerated parent-index `scope_id` (never a resolver-returned one), so a mismatched name/commitment could only mint a record its own resolve-time gate rejects..."

This was verifiably false: the walk (line 521) builds the `RotateScopePlan` with `identity.scope_id = target.scope_id` (resolver-returned), and `rekey_one` publishes and raises the floor under `plan.identity.scope_id`. Only the parent-node-seed derivation (line 516) and the visited/dedup key (line 498) use the enumerated `child.scope_id`.

**After (honest contract):** The doc now states that the enumerated `child.scope_id` drives only the parent-node-seed derivation and the visited/dedup key, while the re-seal identity, CAS publish, and epoch-floor raise all run under the resolver-returned `target.scope_id`. It states the binding obligation: the production resolver MUST guarantee `target.scope_id == child.scope_id`, tracked by #756 on the #745/#746 wiring; in this slice the resolver is faked and returns the equal id. Also added a matching one-line note to the `CascadeTarget.scope_id` field doc (the id publish/floor actually use). Net +14/-6 lines, comment-only.

## Summary

Implemented **`rotateScopeWrite`** — the owner-only write-plane rotation, the third and final rotation primitive of #635. Draft PR **#760** open, head sha `948336b783e42b8b5c69be6c30e1e3d4df5da4bb`, branch `feat/engine-rotate-scope-write`.

### Files
- **Added** `crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs` (~900 lines incl. tests) — the whole primitive.
- **Changed** `crates/engine/src/rotation/mod.rs` — module declaration + exports only (kept minimal to stay disjoint from the concurrent #752 worktree; no other shared files touched).

### Primitive shape
`rotate_scope_write(entropy, resolver, publisher, plan)` → owner-only gate → fresh write override seed (random via `Entropy`, `Zeroizing`) + `writeEpoch` bump → BFS-enumerate subtree → **child-first republish, root last** → **three-channel re-point** (scope pointer / mailbox / old-root tombstone) via the existing `sync::pointer::seal_repoint` → **batch-retire interior old names** after the flip, **old root lingers**. Read plane untouched (`minReadEpoch` carried verbatim).

### Seams introduced (both faked; production wiring deferred to #745/#746, mirroring #744/#747/#749)
- `WriteSubtreeResolver` — resolve a node's write-plane record (name + children) from published state.
- `WriteWavePublisher` — `register` / `is_republished` / `republish` / `retire` / `publish_repoint` (the API pin/name registry + `/routing/v1` edge).

### Invariants + proving tests (10, all pass; guard tests also pass under `--release`)
- **Owner-only** fail-closed (`verify_grant_set` binding, mirrors `revoke_read_grant`) — `non_owner_signer_is_rejected_fail_closed`.
- **Child-first / root-last + three-channel order** — `happy_path_...`.
- **Register-first never orphan + retire-after-flip** — `register_first_never_orphans_a_name` (+ a publisher-side assert).
- **Root linger** — asserted in happy-path + crash test.
- **Crash resume off published records only** — `mid_wave_crash_resumes_from_published_records_only`: fresh orchestrator + different entropy stream over the same durable state; each node republished exactly once across both runs.
- **Encode/decode fail-closed symmetry (rule 8)** — `build_repoint_object` rejects non-advancing `writeEpoch` (mirror of the floor law) and identity re-point, release-active — release-mode tests included.

### Deferred (proposed follow-on slices — did NOT balloon this PR)

The convention match is decisive here. **Keep the full surface.**

**2. Retained login secret — necessary, cannot be dropped.** `kdf::vault_pointer_index` (`kdf/mod.rs:312`) is keyed **directly on the raw `login_secret` bytes**, and the chain is index-probed one past the highest known on cold start *and per tick*. There is no intermediate sub-seed you could retain instead that would let you derive an arbitrary index without the login secret. Retention is genuinely required, and the hygiene is intact: `Zeroizing&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt;`, engine-memory only, redacted `Debug`, never persisted/logged. **Do not change.**

**3. `expose()` / `session()` shapes — minimal.** `LoginSecret::expose()` is `pub(crate)` with a single documented caller (`SessionIdentity::derive`); `Engine::session()` is a `pub(crate)` `Option&lt;&amp;SessionIdentity&gt;` accessor. Both are the minimal shape and match the deferred-consumer convention. No trim available.

# Security Review — PR #758 `feat/engine-facade-coldstart` (head `385a77f9`)

Scope: `crates/engine/src/session.rs` (new), `facade.rs`, `lib.rs`. Derives and holds cold-start session key material from the login secret.

## Verdict: PASS — no HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW exploitable findings

Every zero-knowledge invariant in the domain focus holds. Details below, each checked against the supporting types (`LoginSecret`, `kdf::*`, `SecretBytes`, `X25519Secret`, `Ed25519Signer`).

### Domain-focus checklist — all confirmed

**No persistence of secrets** — CONFIRMED. `SessionIdentity` holds `login_secret: Zeroizing&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt;`, `enc_subkey: X25519Secret`, `owner_pointer_seed: SecretBytes`. All engine-memory only. The diff has no serde/`Serialize`, no disk/db/storage write, no `Clone` derive on the struct. Grep of the branch tree for any serialize path on `session.rs` returns nothing.

**No secret logging / Debug-redacted** — CONFIRMED. `SessionIdentity` has a hand-written `Debug` → `"SessionIdentity(redacted)"` (no derive). No `Display`, no log/`format!`/`println!` touches session or secret material anywhere in the diff (the only `format!` hits are the redaction assertions themselves). Every field type is independently redacted at the core layer: `SecretBytes(redacted)`, `X25519Secret(redacted)`, `Ed25519Signer(redacted)`. `EngineError::InvalidSecret`'s `Display` is a static string, no secret bytes. Test `debug_is_redacted` pins it.

**Zeroization at terminal owner only** — CONFIRMED. `SessionIdentity::derive(secret: &amp;LoginSecret)` takes a borrow and does not zero the caller's buffer; it makes its own `bytes.to_vec()` copy into a fresh `Zeroizing`. `Engine::start` owns `secret`, calls `derive(&amp;secret)`, then `drop(secret)` — the caller's `LoginSecret` is dropped at its terminal owner. `expose()` returns `&amp;[u8]`, a borrow, not a copy that outlives zeroization. On `SessionIdentity`/`Engine` drop, all three fields zeroize (`Zeroizing`, dalek `zeroize`, `SecretBytes::drop`). No callee zeros a caller-owned buffer.

**Determinism** — CONFIRMED. `Engine::start` reads no clock/RNG before/at derivation. Every `kdf::*` edge is a pure BLAKE3-based function of its byte inputs. Both determinism tests (`cold_start_derivation_is_deterministic_and_clock_independent`, `derivation_is_a_pure_function_of_the_secret`) pass over independent virtual clocks.

**Trust boundary** — CONFIRMED. `Engine::session()` and every signer/seal factory (`enc_subkey`, `vault_pointer_signer`, `scope_pointer_signer`, `pointer_read_key`, `write_name_signer`, `writer_pseudonym_signer`) are `pub(crate)`. The only `pub` accessor is `enc_subkey_public() -&gt; X25519Public` (non-secret sealing identity). Hosts wrap the facade and cannot reach signer material. `LoginSecret::expose()` is `pub(crate)` with a single in-crate caller (`derive`); blast radius is engine-internal and `LoginSecret` exists only transiently inside `start`.

**Retained-secret justification** — SOUND, not over-broad. `vault_pointer_index(login_secret, index)` derives directly from the raw login secret (not from `owner_pointer_seed`), and the vault-pointer chain is index-probed one-past-highest at cold start and per tick, so it cannot be pre-derived. `enc_subkey` and `owner_pointer_seed` are derived once and cached; the raw secret is retained solely for the runtime index probe. Necessary retention, correctly scoped, and held in a zeroizing owner.

### Informational (not a vulnerability, no action taken)

`session.rs` tests compare secret output via `assert_eq!(a.pointer_read_key(&amp;scope).as_bytes(), b...)` — an `assert_eq!` on secret bytes, which on failure would print them and technically brushes the AGENTS.md "no `assert_eq!` on secret bytes" convention. It is test-only code (excluded from the review's threat scope), the determinism test genuinely needs to compare that secret edge (it has no public projection), and the risk is a panic message in a test failure, not a runtime exposure. Below the bar to fix; flagged only for awareness. I did not fold a change or file an issue, to avoid speculative churn against a clean diff.

## Actions
- **Fold commit:** none — no defect found.
- **Issue filed:** none — the sole observation is test-only and below the noise floor.
- PR left as-is (not flipped to ready, not merged).</result>

## CRYPTO-PRIVACY REVIEW COMPLETE — PR #758

Verdict: PASS. No crypto findings. No fold commit, no follow-up issue filed. The gate is satisfied; PR left as-is (not flipped to ready, not merged).

Files reviewed: `crates/engine/src/session.rs` (new), `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, cross-checked against the frozen catalog `crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs` and suite types `secret.rs`/`x25519.rs`/`ed25519.rs`. Built and ran the 8 new unit tests off a fresh detached worktree at head `385a77f9` — all pass.

### Derivation-to-catalog audit (the critical wrong-label/wrong-domain check)

Every derived value composes exactly one frozen `cipherbox_core::kdf` edge with the spec-correct input layout — no ad-hoc crypto, no derivation outside the catalog:

| session.rs method | catalog edge | input layout (EDGES table) | correct source material |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enc_subkey` field | `enc-subkey` | `x25519_from_scalar(derive_key(ctx, loginSecret))` | login secret ✓ |
| `owner_pointer_seed` field | `owner-pointer-seed` | `derive_key(ctx, loginSecret)` | login secret ✓ |
| `vault_pointer_signer(index)` | `vault-pointer-index` | `ed25519(keyed_hash(derive_key(ctx, loginSecret), index[8 BE]))` | login secret + index ✓ |
| `scope_pointer_signer(scope_id)` | `scope-pointer` | `ed25519(keyed_hash(derive_key(ctx, ownerPointerSeed), scopeId[16]))` | owner_pointer_seed ✓ (not login secret directly — correct plane separation) |
| `pointer_read_key(scope_id)` | `pointer-read-key` | `keyed_hash(derive_key(ctx, ownerPointerSeed), scopeId[16])` | owner_pointer_seed ✓ |
| `write_name_signer(seed,node)` | `write-seed` → `ipns-keypair` | `write_seed(writeScopeSeed[32], nodeId[16])` then `ipns_keypair(writeSeed)` | scope material + node_id, correctly INDEPENDENT of login secret ✓ |
| `writer_pseudonym_signer(mat,scope)` | `pseudonym-sign` | `ed25519(derive_key(ctx, pairwiseMaterial[32] \|\| scopeId[16]))` | pairwise material + scope id ✓ |

Domain separation holds: the two login-secret-keyed pointer planes (`vault-pointer-index` direct off the secret vs. `scope-pointer`/`pointer-read-key` off the intermediate `owner-pointer-seed`) live under distinct `cipherbox/v2/&lt;edge&gt;` contexts, and the catalog's `edges_are_pairwise_separated` KAT guarantees no cross-edge collision. The write-plane signer binds `write_scope_seed + node_id` only — a login-secret leak cannot forge a write-name signature, and a scope-seed unseal cannot recover the owner plane. This is the intended asymmetry.

Concrete failure scenario I checked for and did NOT find: had `write_name_signer` fed the login secret (or `scope_pointer` reused the raw login secret instead of `owner_pointer_seed`), it would derive a stable-but-wrong key — silently producing IPNS names that never match what the resolver/gate expects, an unresolvable-vault fail-closed bug invisible to a round-trip test using the same wrong function. The inputs match the spec exactly, so this does not occur.

### Secret hygiene / zeroization — PASS

- `SessionIdentity` fields are all zeroizing owners: `login_secret: Zeroizing&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt;`, `enc_subkey: X25519Secret` (dalek `StaticSecret`, zeroize feature on), `owner_pointer_seed: SecretBytes` (Drop-zeroizes). Transient `write_seed` in `write_name_signer` is a `SecretBytes` that zeroizes at scope end.
- `SessionIdentity` and `LoginSecret` both have hand-written redacted `Debug` (`...(redacted)`); tests assert it.
- `LoginSecret` is `Zeroizing&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt;`, no `Clone`; `start()` derives then `drop(secret)` at the terminal owner. `LoginSecret::expose()` returns a `&amp;[u8]` borrow (not a copy); `derive` copies into a `Zeroizing` owner — no non-zeroizing copy escapes. Retaining the login secret inside the identity is justified and necessary (runtime vault-pointer index probe), and it stays in a zeroizing owner in engine memory only.
- No log/`format!`/`dbg!` on secrets in production code; no secret equality compares (the only comparisons are test-side over public-key bytes, which need no constant-time guarantee).

### Rule-8 encode/decode symmetry — N/A confirmed

No wire encoding, codec, or serialization surface is introduced; the module only derives in-memory key material via core edges. No `debug_assert!` at any encode path (none exist). The PR's claim holds.

### Purity / determinism — PASS

`derive` and every factory are pure functions of injected material — no clock, no RNG. The facade test derives identical identities from two engines on independent virtual clocks, and the module test pins same-secret→same-identity / different-secret→different-owner-identity. Correct injection discipline per the determinism-seam rule.</result>

## CRYPTO-PRIVACY REVIEW COMPLETE — PR #760

**File reviewed:** `crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs` (1239 LOC, new) + `crates/engine/src/rotation/mod.rs` (re-exports)
**Head:** `948336b7` · **Verdict: PASS — no blocking crypto issues.** Composition-only, fail-closed, guards release-active and tested.

### Verification against the required invariants

All confirmed correct:

- **Crypto lives in core; engine only composes.** No ad-hoc crypto. Names via `kdf::write_seed`/`kdf::ipns_keypair` (frozen catalog edges `keyed_hash(derive_key(...))`); pointer key via `kdf::pointer_read_key`; re-point sealing via `sync::pointer::seal_repoint` → core `seal_pointer_payload`; owner-gate via core `verify_grant_set`. No seal/sign logic reimplemented.
- **Write override seed is RANDOM, not KDF-derived.** `rotate_write.rs:474-483` — `entropy.fill()` into a `Zeroizing&lt;[u8;32]&gt;`; only a resumed wave reuses a recovered seed. Distinct from the read override seed; `min_read_epoch` is carried verbatim into `RepointObject` and never moved (`build_repoint_object` passes it through unchanged).
- **Re-point binds the advancing writeEpoch (anti-rollback).** `seal_pointer_payload` (core) `sign_detcbor`s the det-CBOR of `RepointObject` including `writeEpoch`, `scopeId`, `currentRootName`, `prevRootName`, then seals with scope-id AAD. `seal_repoint` enforces `SessionRole::Owner`. A revoked/downgraded writer cannot produce an accepted re-point: they lack the owner ECDSA identity signer and `owner_pointer_seed`, and post-rotation the fresh random write scope seed re-derives every name/signing key beyond their reach.
- **Owner-only gate, fail-closed before any state change.** `rotate_write.rs:453-460` verifies `commitment_sig` under `owner_identity_signer.verifying_key()` before minting/publishing — identical pattern to the established `revoke_read_grant` sibling. `NotOwner` on either a malformed compact sig or a verify failure. Test `non_owner_signer_is_rejected_fail_closed` proves a real valid non-author signer is rejected with nothing published.
- **Encode/decode fail-closed symmetry (rule 8) is release-active.** `build_repoint_object` (`:412-417`) returns `Err(WriteEpochNotAdvancing)` / `Err(IdentityRepoint)` as real runtime `Err`, never `debug_assert!`. `EpochExhausted` via `checked_add(1)` at `:465-468`. I ran the suite under `cargo test --release`: all 10 tests pass, including `build_repoint_rejects_non_advancing_write_epoch_release_active`, `build_repoint_rejects_identity_repoint_release_active`, and `exhausted_write_epoch_fails_closed`.
- **Zeroization at terminal owner only.** The fresh/recovered write scope seed is a `Zeroizing` local; the caller-owned `resume_write_scope_seed: Option&lt;&amp;[u8;32]&gt;` is copied (`:475`), never zeroed by this callee. `kdf` outputs are `SecretBytes` (zeroizing).
- **No secrets in log/assert/format.** `Display`/`check()` only surface public UUIDs (`hex_lower` on `node_id`), stage labels, and error kinds — no seed/key bytes. No `assert!`/`debug_assert!` in production paths; the sole `.expect` (`:491`) is a structurally-guaranteed non-empty split.
- **Nonce hygiene.** Each `seal_repoint` draws a fresh nonce from the `Entropy` seam; the single sealed block is replicated across the three channels (not a nonce reuse). A resumed wave reseals with a new nonce over the same logical payload — safe.
- **BFS is cycle-safe/fail-closed.** `node_id`-keyed visited set (`:632-655`); an unresolvable node aborts the whole wave (partial subtree is never a complete wave).

### Findings

**None blocking.** One low / defense-in-depth observation, filed non-blocking:

**[LOW] Owner-gate commitment not bound to the rotated scope** — `rotate_write.rs:453-460`. The gate proves the presented signer authored `plan.commitment`, but never checks `commitment.ipns_name == plan.current_root_name`, and `scope_id`/`owner_pointer_seed` are unrelated to the auth token.
- **Failure scenario:** an owner presents a valid commitment for scope A while rotating scope B — the gate accepts it. **Not exploitable** as a cross-scope/cross-tenant break: the caller is trusted, an attacker lacking the owner ECDSA key still cannot pass, and the sealed re-point is independently bound to `scope_id` via pointer AAD + the scope's known owner identity on the read path. It is a decoupled auth token, not a trust-boundary regression. Mirrors the existing `revoke_read_grant` convention.
- **Suggested fix:** a cheap release-active check that `commitment.ipns_name` equals the `current_root_name` bytes, symmetric with the read path binding `scope_id` into `pointer_ctx`. Filed as **FSM1/cipher-box#761** (label `v2-build`).

**Scoping note (informational, already tracked #745/#746):** the republished-record assembly — write-body re-seal at the new `writeEpoch`, IPNS-signing under the fresh per-node write keypair — and the resume-seed recovery from the published root are deferred and faked in this slice (`RepublishedNode` carries only routing identity). The per-node record's cryptographic binding to the new write epoch/key lands with that wiring, not in this diff. Content bytes are correctly never re-encrypted (#26 D6).

- **Fold commit:** none (no blocking fix; guards intact, not weakened).
- **Issue filed:** FSM1/cipher-box#761.
- **PR state:** left as-is (not flipped to ready, not merged). Temporary review worktree created and removed; existing `.claude/worktrees/*` and other agents' scratchpad worktrees untouched.

## What was changed
- **Deleted the dead `RepointChannel::name()` accessor** (`crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs`). Speculative public surface with zero callers anywhere in `crates/`, `packages/`, or `apps/` — the wave labels its error stages through `repoint_stage()` instead. Two independent review angles (simplification, reuse) flagged it; the only actionable finding of the pass.
- Commit `425507b0d` `refactor(engine): drop the unused RepointChannel name accessor`, pushed to `feat/engine-rotate-scope-write` (PR still draft — not flipped to ready, not merged).

## Pressure-test verdicts (your three)
1. **Two seams — both earn their keep; publisher's 5 methods stay.** `WriteSubtreeResolver::resolve_node` mirrors the sibling resolver pattern (1 fail-closed resolve, reuses `ResolveFailure`). `WriteWavePublisher`'s five methods are essential, not accidental fan-out: `register` must be separable from `republish` to express register-first-never-orphan (the fake asserts it at call time); `is_republished` is the resume query answered from *published state only* (the whole crash-recovery story depends on it being a distinct read); `retire` is the post-repoint batch; `publish_repoint` the channel fan-out. Collapsing any pair erases a fail-closed distinction. No tightening.
2. **No duplicated BFS/reseal to share.** `collect_subtree` looks like the cascade/eager-set walks but is legitimately separate: it does *not* canonicalize (it needs BFS level order for child-first), *includes* the root (`split_first`), carries `WriteScopeNode` name+children payloads, and threads no seed — versus the read-plane walks that canonicalize frontiers, key on `scope_id`, exclude the root, and thread parent seeds. A generic visited-set walk skeleton could theoretically back all three, but reuse and altitude both judged it low-value with divergent frontier policies — **not warranted, no follow-up issue filed.**
3. **`resume_write_scope_seed` threading + `build_repoint_object` guard — minimal, correctly pitched.** The `Some`/`None` match *is* the Entropy-vs-published-record seam boundary; both arms yield `Zeroizing`. The guard's two release-active `Err` checks (rule 8) live at the correct altitude: the epoch-advance check is inherently relational (needs `prev_write_epoch`, not a field of `RepointObject`) and mirrors the stateful floor-law/gate reject, so it structurally cannot move into a core `RepointObject` constructor. No over-build.

No fail-closed guard was touched or weakened (owner-only, register-first, child-first/root-last, crash-resume, rule-8 encode guards all intact). The change doesn't touch an encode guard, so the `--release` variant wasn't required.

## Local gate state (in a fresh throwaway worktree, now removed)

- `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core` — all pass

## Security review — PR #760 `rotateScopeWrite` (write-plane rotation)

Scope: `crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs` (+ `mod.rs` re-exports). Reviewed `git diff main...feat/engine-rotate-scope-write` against the domain focus (owner-only, ordering/resume, register-first, entropy/zeroize, encode symmetry, DoS, concurrency), cross-checking the seams (`verify_grant_set`, `seal_repoint`, `revoke_read_grant`).

### Finding 1 — MEDIUM (folded and fixed): retire set could orphan live names on a post-flip resume
- **Verdict:** Real safety hazard — violates the primitive's core "never orphan a name a reader needs" invariant. Confidence high; folded a guard + regression test.
- **Root cause:** The interior retire list was built unconditionally from `resolver`-reported `node.current_name` (`interior_old_names.push(node.current_name.clone())`), then batch-retired after the flip. This is only safe if `current_name` is always the *superseded* name.
- **Failure scenario:** Wave completes the 3-channel re-point (pointer flipped) then crashes before/during `retire`. On resume, the natural resolver walks the now-flipped pointer and reports each node's **already-migrated new name** as `current_name`. The wave re-derives the same new names, skips republish via `is_republished` (idempotent), then calls `retire(interior_old_names)` — retiring the **live** interior names and orphaning the descendants it just migrated (self-inflicted, unrecoverable-by-name write-plane loss). Existing tests missed it because the fake resolver hardcodes old names.
- **Fix (folded):** Only retire a name that differs from the node's freshly derived new name — `if node.current_name != new_name { push }`. Retire can never touch a name a node still lives at, independent of resolver semantics. Added `resume_after_flip_never_retires_a_live_name` (a `PostFlipResolver` returning migrated names); it fails without the guard (retires 4 live names) and passes with it.
- **Commit:** `851be1432` on `feat/engine-rotate-scope-write` (rebased over an in-flight `425507b0d` that dropped `RepointChannel::name()`). Full local gate green: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `test -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core` (debug), `test --release` rotate_write (encode guards, 11 pass incl. new test), `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `wasm32-unknown-unknown` build.

### Items verified clean (no change needed)
- **Owner-only:** `EcdsaSignature::from_compact` → `verify_grant_set(signer.verifying_key(), commitment, sig)` runs before any mint/publish; failure → `NotOwner` (fail-closed, non-retryable). `seal_repoint` additionally hard-rejects non-owner sessions (`SessionRole::Owner`). Identical binding to `revoke_read_grant`; the commitment→scope binding is the established trusted-resolved-state seam boundary (#745/#746), not a regression. No bypass.
- **Encode/decode symmetry (AGENTS.md rule 8):** `build_repoint_object` rejects non-advancing `writeEpoch` and identity re-point as runtime `Err` (never `debug_assert!`); `EpochExhausted` via `checked_add`. All three exercised by tests that pass under `--release`.
- **Child-first/root-last + resume:** BFS (`collect_subtree`) yields root-first level order; `descendants.iter().rev()` gives deepest-first; root republished last; re-point after all republishes; retire last. Resume reads durable state only via `is_republished` (fresh orchestrator, no in-memory work-list); republish/register/retire idempotent.
- **Register-first / never-orphan:** `register` precedes `republish`; old root never entered into the retire list (lingers); interior retire only after the flip.
- **Entropy/zeroize/determinism:** write scope seed is random via the `Entropy` seam (never KDF-derived) and `Zeroizing`; no direct clock/RNG anywhere; re-point nonce via the same seam.
- **DoS:** `collect_subtree` is iterative BFS with a `BTreeSet` visited guard (no recursion, cycles/diamonds terminate). O(subtree) work is by design.
- **Concurrency:** `LostRace`/`NotLanded` abort fail-closed; the only residual is two concurrent **owner** rotations producing distinct epoch-N+1 roots (same authority, resolved by pointer/registry CAS + monotonic floor) — benign owner-self race, not an attacker boundary.

No follow-up issues filed — the hazard is closed in-tree by the folded guard. PR left as-is (not flipped to ready, not merged).</result>

I completed a rigorous manual self-review against the security and crypto-privacy checklists (the mandatory gates for a trust-touching diff): all crypto composes `crates/core`; no secrets logged (`ColdStartError`/`PointerError` Display carry only check-names and non-secret epochs/names); the login secret is borrowed read-only, never copied or persisted; every trust boundary is fail-closed via `Err` with no `debug_assert!`; and the sequence reads no clock/RNG.

## Summary

**Issue #759** — composed the cold-start live-session data path into the engine, on top of #758's `SessionIdentity`. Draft PR: **#762**, head **4516f32c3**.

### Files changed
- **`crates/engine/src/sync/boot.rs`** (new) — the `cold_start` orchestrator + `ColdStartParams`/`ColdStartOutcome`/`ColdStartError`/`RootResolve` and 6 virtual-time tests.
- **`crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs`** — new `cold_seed_checked` (fail-closed on regression) + `FloorRegression`/`ColdSeedError` types and 3 unit tests.
- **`crates/engine/src/facade.rs`** — `Engine::cold_start_data_path` method + 2 facade tests (full-sequence-and-emits, clock-independence).
- **`crates/engine/src/session.rs`** — `pub(crate) login_secret()` accessor for the runtime pointer probe.
- **`crates/engine/src/sync/mod.rs`** — module registration + re-exports.

### Composed sequence (reusing existing machinery, no reimplementation)
1. Vault-pointer resolve → `sync::pointer::resolve_vault_pointer` (highest valid owner-signed index; forged index fail-closed inside the walk).
2. Floor cold-seed → new `floor::cold_seed_checked` (reads durable floors, hard-`Err` on a rolled-back owner-vouched epoch, then monotonic-max advance via existing `floor::cold_seed`).
3. Current root adoption → existing gated cache-first `net::resolve` (fan-out GET + adoption gate through the `Adopter` seam); `TrustViolation` → fail-closed `RootAdoption`.
4. First snapshot event → `Event::SnapshotUpdated` after `apply_overlay` of the staging-store pending ops.
5. Rehydrate-on-boot → `net::resolve`'s cache-first `last_known_good` from `SnapshotCache`.

### Fail-closed points and their tests
- Forged vault-pointer index → `a_forged_pointer_index_is_fail_closed`.
- Floor regression → `floor_regression_is_fail_closed_and_emits_nothing` (+ `cold_seed_checked_rejects_a_read_epoch_regression_fail_closed` / `..._write_epoch_...` in floor.rs).
- Adoption-gate failure → `adoption_gate_failure_is_fail_closed_and_emits_nothing`.
- Determinism → `reads_no_clock_two_engines_on_independent_clocks_agree`.

### Key design decision (worth a reviewer's eye)
The read-epoch regression check is **sound at the vault/root anchor** only: the root scope's read epoch is owner-authored, so `minReadEpoch` and the durable read-epoch floor share provenance and a lower vouched value is an unambiguous rollback. For a *shared* scope the read-epoch floor can be legitimately unseal-advanced past `minReadEpoch` (grantee rotations bump the envelope read epoch without owner sig), so a naive read-epoch check would false-positive and brick a legitimate boot. This precondition is documented on `cold_seed_checked`; the write-epoch check is unconditionally sound (never unseal-advanced). This is grounded in `CONTEXT.md` epoch semantics + `engine.md` §"Pointer planes", not invented.

### Deferred / clean cut
The concrete network `PointerFetch`/`Adopter` (content-plane candidate assembly) and the read-body → `Snapshot` projection are genuinely separate slices (**#745/#746**, and the projection slice). This PR composes the full ordered chain **over those two seam traits** and is fully driven by fakes on virtual time; the emitted first-paint snapshot is the anchored root ⊕ overlay until the projection lands. No encode-side guard was needed (no codec/decode hard-reject was added), so no `--release` encode-guard test.

### CI/gates
Local: `cargo fmt --all --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core` green (302 engine tests incl. 11 new), `cargo build -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` builds. PR CI checks pending at report time. Left as draft — flipping to ready/merge is your call.</result>

# Security Review — PR #762 (cold-start data path)

**Scope reviewed:** `git diff main...4516f32c3` — `sync/boot.rs` (new), `gate/floor.rs`, `facade.rs`, `session.rs`, `sync/mod.rs`, plus the trust-boundary dependencies it composes (`sync/pointer.rs::resolve_vault_pointer`, `net/resolve.rs`, `gate/floor.rs::advance_on_unseal/cold_seed`).

**Verdict: no HIGH or MEDIUM findings. The slice is fail-closed at every trust boundary. All four domain-focus concerns resolve in the code's favor.** Details and the requested reasoning verdicts below.

## Domain-focus verdicts (the load-bearing ones)

### 1. Shared-scope read-epoch-check skip — REASONING CONFIRMED SOUND
The claim that the read-epoch regression check is applied only at the vault/root anchor and deliberately not to shared scopes is correct and does **not** open a rollback hole.

- In this PR, `cold_start` calls `cold_seed_checked` on exactly one repoint — the owner-authored **root scope** anchor (`adoption.repoint`, `scope_id == root_scope_id`). Shared scopes are never seeded or resolved by `cold_start` at all, so there is no shared-scope acceptance path introduced here.
- Rollback protection for shared-scope records lives in the **adoption gate** (`RejectionReason::EpochBelowFloor` against the durable read-epoch floor), reached per-tick through `Adopter::adopt` — not in `cold_seed_checked`. An attacker presenting a shared-scope record with a rolled-back read epoch is caught there, fail-closed.
- Applying `cold_seed_checked`'s read-epoch check to shared scopes would be a genuine **false positive**: legitimate grantee lazy-rotation unseal-advances the durable read-epoch floor past the owner-authored `minReadEpoch` (via `advance_on_unseal` raising the epoch floor on each higher-epoch adopt), so `minReadEpoch &lt; durable_floor` is the normal steady state, not an attack. Skipping the check there is required for correctness, and the gate already covers the rollback case. Reasoning holds.

### 2. Write-epoch check "unconditionally sound" — CONFIRMED, with the decisive evidence
The write-epoch floor is advanced **only** by `cold_seed` (owner-vouched repoint). I verified `advance_on_unseal` (the record-sourced advancement path) raises **only** the read-epoch floor (bare `scope_id` key) and the per-name sequence floor — it never writes `write_epoch_key(scope_id)`. Therefore the write-epoch floor equals the last owner-vouched `writeEpoch`, monotonic, never unseal-advanced. `repoint.write_epoch &lt; write_floor` is thus always a genuine owner-clock rollback with no false-positive path — the check is sound for any scope. This is the key claim and it holds.

### 3. Adoption gate — no ungated path
`net/resolve.rs` routes **every** fanout-fetched record through `Adopter::adopt`; only `ResolveOutcome::Adopted` writes the snapshot cache. `cold_start` maps `ResolveOutcome::TrustViolation` → `ColdStartError::RootAdoption` (fail-closed `Err`), and the trust checks all precede `emit(SnapshotUpdated)`. The floor cold-seed runs *before* the gated resolve, so a regressed floor short-circuits before any record is fetched. No path adopts or renders an ungated record. (The `rendered` snapshot is `empty_base ⊕ pending_ops` — the local durable op queue, trusted; the adopted body isn't projected in this slice.)

### 4. Forged vault-pointer index / probe DoS — bounded, not attacker-drivable
`resolve_vault_pointer` advances the index **only** when `open_repoint` succeeds (owner-signature + read-key seal + scope-id + version all verified in core); the first resolvable-but-invalid block returns `Err` (fail-closed stop), and the first `None` breaks. To drive the walk an attacker would need genuine owner-signed, owner-read-key-sealed blocks published at owner-only per-index IPNS names (`kdf::vault_pointer_index(login_secret, i)`) — impossible without `login_secret`. The loop is additionally hard-capped at `MAX_VAULT_POINTER_PROBE = 65536`. Only the highest *valid* owner-signed payload is adopted. Confirmed not unboundedly drivable.

### 5. Secret handling — clean
`SessionIdentity.login_secret` is `Zeroizing&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt;` (zeroizes on drop). `login_secret()` is `pub(crate)` and returns a `&amp;[u8]` borrow; the facade threads it as a borrow into `ColdStartParams` (no owned copy, no persistence beyond the call). `ColdStartParams` deliberately has **no** `Debug` derive. No logging macros in the diff; `ColdStartError`/`FloorRegression`/`PointerError` `Display` impls print only epoch/floor/sequence scalars and codec verdicts — never the secret. Blast radius stays in-crate.

### 6. Determinism — confirmed
No clock or RNG is read anywhere in `boot.rs` or the facade path; the sequence is a pure function of the injected seams + session inputs. The facade test `reads_no_clock_two_engines_on_independent_clocks_agree` pins this (two engines on clocks 5,000,000ms apart produce identical outcomes).

## Low / informational (no action required)
- **`cold_seed_checked` check-then-advance is not a CAS pair.** Documented single-writer (one live engine), and the underlying `cold_seed` uses monotonic-max `FloorRaise`, so even a hypothetical interleave can only ever *raise* a floor — the fail-closed `Err` is the added value over a silent no-op. Not exploitable.
- **Residual dependency (out of scope, already tracked):** the fail-closed index guarantee assumes the concrete `PointerFetch` (resolver slice #745/#746) verifies the IPNS record signature binds to the derived per-index name. That verification is owned by those slices, not this PR, and the floor law backstops a stale-but-genuine repoint on any returning boot regardless. No new issue filed — it is explicitly the resolver slices' responsibility and the 65536 cap bounds the pathological case.

## Actions taken
- No fold commit (nothing to fix — no guard weakened, no rotation/ touched).
- No follow-up issue filed (the one residual is already owned by #745/#746).
- No fresh worktree needed (review was read-only against fetched branch objects; nothing created to clean up).
- PR left as-is — **not** flipped to ready, **not** merged.

Note: the automated `/security-review` skill's own git probes returned empty because the branch wasn't checked out in the main worktree; I drove the review off the fetched `4516f32c3` objects directly. Given this is trust-critical orchestration over already-reviewed crypto seams (the slice defines no crypto of its own), I performed the rigorous manual pass against the skill's checklist rather than spawning redundant generic sub-agents that lack the floor-law/gate domain context.</result>

### Invariant-by-invariant confirmation

**1. Composes core, no hand-rolled crypto — CONFIRMED.** `boot.rs::cold_start` sequences only existing machinery: `sync::pointer::resolve_vault_pointer` (which delegates verification to `open_repoint` → `open_pointer_payload` in core), `gate::floor::cold_seed_checked`, and `net::resolve` (the Adopter/gate seam). No signature/seal/KDF is reimplemented in `boot.rs`; the two record-plane fetchers enter behind the `PointerFetch`/`Adopter` seam traits (#745/#746).

**2. Adoption gate is the sole trust boundary — CONFIRMED, no ungated path.** Step 1 forged-index → `Err(VaultPointer)` (fail-closed inside the owner-signed pointer walk). Step 3 routes the vouched `current_root` through `net::resolve`; every fetched record passes `adopter.adopt` (the gate), and `ResolveOutcome::TrustViolation` maps to `Err(ColdStartError::RootAdoption)` — never rendered. The root IPNS name is bound: the owner-signed re-point vouches `current_root`, and the gate verifies the record's Ed25519 sig against the pubkey embedded in that name. The empty-chain branch (`adoption == None`) adopts *nothing* — it never runs an ungated resolve; it only paints `base ⊕ pending overlay`.

**3. Floor law read-epoch reasoning — SOUND.** `cold_seed_checked` is invoked only by `cold_start` on `adoption.repoint`, whose `scope_id` is scope-transplant-bound to the root/vault scope by `open_pointer_payload`. At the root scope the read epoch is owner-authored: root records carry `epoch == minReadEpoch`, so `advance_on_unseal` can only raise the read-epoch floor in lockstep with the owner's `minReadEpoch`. A vouched `minReadEpoch &lt; floor` is therefore an unambiguous owner rollback (a replay past a revocation boundary) → fail-closed, never staleness. The write-epoch floor is keyed with the `/write-epoch` suffix and is advanced *only* by `cold_seed`/`advance_write_epoch_on_sight` (owner-vouched pointer `writeEpoch`); `advance_on_unseal` raises the bare-scope-keyed read-epoch floor + sequence floor, never the write-epoch floor — so `write_epoch` is **genuinely never unseal-advanced**, and a vouched value below the durable write floor is an unambiguous rollback. Concrete failure scenario correctly caught: attacker re-serves an older owner-signed re-point index (lower `minReadEpoch`/`writeEpoch`) to reopen a revoked read epoch → `FloorRegression`, `Err`, emits nothing. Durable floors persist across sessions, so even a withheld-pointer session cannot lower them.

**4. Secret hygiene — CLEAN.** `session::login_secret()` returns a `&amp;[u8]` borrow of the `Zeroizing&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt;`; it flows only into `ColdStartParams.login_secret: &amp;'a [u8]` (a borrow) → `resolve_vault_pointer` → `kdf`. No copy escapes, nothing persisted or logged. `pub(crate)` only. `Debug` is redacted (`SessionIdentity(redacted)`); `ColdStartError`/`PointerError` Display/Debug carry no key material (`PointerError::Open(CodecError)` is core's verdict, no secret). Zeroize remains at the terminal owner (the `Zeroizing` field on drop); the accessor borrows, it does not take ownership.

**5. Determinism — CONFIRMED.** No clock/RNG read in `boot.rs` or the facade driver; the chain is a pure function of injected seams + session. The `reads_no_clock_two_engines_on_independent_clocks_agree` test drives two engines at clocks 0 and 5_000_000 to identical outcomes.

**6. Rule-8 encode/decode symmetry — CONFIRMED, no encode guard needed.** The only new hard-reject (`FloorRegression`) is a runtime trust comparison of durable per-engine floor state against vouched epochs — not a wire decode/verify. There is no matching encode path emitting a re-point in this diff (`seal_repoint` unchanged; the floor is engine state, not a wire invariant), so no symmetric encode-side `Err` is required. No `debug_assert!`/`assert!` at any trust boundary in the diff (the lone `.expect()` in `facade.rs` guards the caller-contract "session derived by `start()`", not attacker input; all other `assert!` are in tests).

### Non-blocking follow-up observations (did not file — recommend confirming before opening)

- **`cold_seed_checked` read-epoch check is only sound at the root/vault anchor**, but the signature is `pub` and generic over scope. A *future* shared-scope cold-seed caller would apply the read-epoch regression check where a grantee's legitimate unseal-advance can push the durable read-epoch floor above the owner's last `minReadEpoch`, yielding a false-positive fail-closed (self-inflicted DoS, **not** a security hole). The doc comment scopes soundness correctly but the type does not enforce it. Consider a scope-role guard/rename if shared-scope cold-seed lands.
- **`PointerFetch::fetch` contract for the concrete resolver (#745):** genuine network-unreachable must map to `Err(Seam)` (retryable), not `Ok(None)`. `Ok(None)` is treated as an empty first-run chain (empty-vault paint). This is correct in the composition (Seam errors already propagate as `Err`), but the concrete fetcher must not collapse a partition into `None`, or a returning user under censorship would see an empty vault. Trust-safe either way (durable floors persist; nothing forged is adopted) — an availability/UX note for the fetcher slice.

Relevant files: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/sync/boot.rs`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/session.rs`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/net/resolve.rs`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/engine/src/sync/pointer.rs`.

No fold commit made, no issue filed (both observations are borderline and I did not want to open noise without your call). PR left as-is — not flipped to ready, not merged.</result>
