## CRYPTO / PRIVACY REVIEW — PR #769 (read-grant creation)

Files reviewed on branch `feat/engine-grant-creation` (worktree `agent-a5f78fc7648c040f8`):
- `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs` (new, 778 lines)
- `crates/engine/src/grants/mod.rs` (exports)
- Composed primitives traced: `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs`, `crates/engine/src/mailbox/mod.rs`, `crates/core/src/payload/mailbox.rs`, `crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs`, `crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs`

---

### [HIGH] Mailbox idempotency key leaks the sharing graph to the untrusted server

Location: `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs:418`

```rust
let idempotency_key = format!("grant:{}", hex_lower(&amp;grantee.scope_id));
```

Invariant at risk: zero-knowledge / no grantee-linkable identifier to the untrusted mailbox. The API's own DTO fixes the contract (`apps/api/src/mailbox/dto/mailbox.dto.ts:34`, `openapi.json:989`, entity comment `mailbox-message.entity.ts:20`): the `idempotencyKey` **MUST be a high-entropy per-message random value** because the server stores `idempotencyScope = sha256(senderPk : idempotencyKey)` (`mailbox.service.ts:264`), and "a low-entropy key would let a server-side observer brute-force the sender→recipient edge."

This key is neither high-entropy nor per-message: it is a deterministic function of the folder's `scope_id` alone.

Exploit / leak:
- `scope_id` is otherwise hidden from the server — the public `ipnsName` is a one-way derivation of `write_seed(write_scope_seed, scope_id)`, so it does not reveal `scope_id`. This is the one plaintext channel that exposes folder identity.
- Co-grantee correlation (no brute force needed): sharing the **same folder** to two recipients yields the **same** `idempotencyKey` → identical `idempotencyScope` stored on two different `recipient_public_key` rows. The server reads directly from stored columns that "recipient A and recipient B were both granted the same folder by the same (hidden) sender" — a concrete sharing-graph edge.
- Edge confirmation: because the digest input is low-entropy/enumerable, an observer who guesses a `(senderPk, scope_id)` pair can confirm the sender→recipient edge, exactly the attack the DTO warns about.

Fix direction: do not derive the key from `scope_id`. Best: draw a random idempotency key from the `Entropy` seam and persist it with the queued op so retries reuse it (satisfies high-entropy + retry-dedup + determinism seam). If a deterministic key is required, key off the per-recipient **blinded `tag`** (already computed at line 289; 32 bytes, high-entropy, differs per recipient so no cross-recipient equality): `format!("grant:{}", hex_lower(&amp;tag))`. Never `scope_id`. Add a test asserting two grants of the same folder to different recipients produce different idempotency keys.

---

### [MEDIUM] Reparented descendant scope roots are not re-keyed to the fresh grantee seed (grantee cannot descend into nested sub-scopes)

Location: `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs:313-374` (mint with fresh random seed + reparent) vs. the convergence sweep at `:251-265` (runs *before* the fresh seed exists).

Invariant at risk: descent correctness under the ascent-link model. To descend into a child scope root, a reader needs `node_seed(parent_scope_seed, child_scope_id)` to open the child's ascent link. The rotation cascade handles exactly this: when a parent seed changes it re-seals each child's ascent link under `node_seed(parent_fresh_seed, child_scope_id)` (`cascade.rs:525-529`, with an explicit "threaded top-down, never from the descendant's stale link" comment).

Grant creation mints a **fresh random** parent seed (`:316-319`) but the descendants are only touched by the pre-mint `sweep_pass`, which re-seals them at their **existing** `parent_node_seed`, not under the not-yet-generated fresh seed. The reparented descendants (`:367-374`) are moved into the new child index but their ascent links still bind the old derivation. Result: the grantee, holding only the fresh seed, derives `node_seed(fresh_seed, child_id)` and cannot open the child ascent links.

Assessment: this is **fail-safe (under-share), not a secrecy break** — the fresh seed correctly prevents any upward/historical over-share, and children simply remain unreadable to the grantee. But it is inconsistent with the reparent + convergence work this function does over those same descendants (which implies they are meant to be reachable). Confirm whether descent re-keying is intentionally deferred to the production resolver/publisher wiring (#745/#746) or is a genuine gap; if deferred, it should be tracked with a dependency edge, since the current mint publishes a share whose nested content the grantee can't reach.

Fix direction (if in-scope): after minting the fresh seed, re-seal each descendant scope root's ascent link under `node_seed(fresh_override_seed, descendant.scope_id)` and publish them (mirroring `cascade.rs:525-553`), rather than only moving index entries.

---

### [LOW] HPKE ephemeral scalar for the share pointer is not zeroized

Location: `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs:413-416` (`let mut ephemeral = [0u8; 32]; entropy.fill(...)`).

The ephemeral scalar is the terminal owner's transient secret; while live it can reconstruct the HPKE shared secret for the sealed pointer. It is left un-zeroized on the stack. This matches the existing pattern in `reseal.rs` (the `fill::&lt;32&gt;` ephemerals there are also not wiped), so it is consistent, not a regression — but per rule 7 (zeroize at the terminal owner) the mint path owns this buffer and should wrap it in `Zeroizing`. Low impact (pointer payload is low-sensitivity: scope name + display name).

---

## Invariants verified clean

- Fresh random override seed per grant: `entropy.fill(override_seed)` into a `Zeroizing` buffer (`:316-319`), threaded to `reseal_scope_root` with `read_epoch: 1`, `prev: None` (`:330-345`). Never KDF-derived, never a parent seed. Correct.
- KDF edge catalog only: `write_seed`, `ipns_keypair`, `blinded_tag`, `pseudonym_sign`, `node_seed`, `structure_key`, `write_key` — all via `cipherbox_core::kdf` typed edges; no ad-hoc crypto in the engine or TS. `node_seed(seed, scope_id)` threading in the cascade is the correct top-down form.
- Primitives unchanged: XChaCha20-Poly1305 (`seal`), BLAKE3 tree KDF, X25519+HPKE (`seal_grant_blob`/`seal_owner_blob`/`seal_ascent_link`/`seal_mailbox_payload`), Ed25519 pseudonym + secp256k1 identity. Composed, not reimplemented.
- ipnsName derived, footgun closed: name is derived from `write_seed(write_scope_seed, scope_id)` → `ipns_keypair` → `IpnsName` (`:273-277`); `GranteeScopePlan` exposes no name field; the derived `name_bytes` is the single source for the blinded tag, commitment `ipns_name`, scope-root identity, child-index ref, and share pointer. No residual caller-supplied grantee-name path. The recipient re-derives the same name from the resolved record, and the tag/commitment bind it — verified against the tests at `:681-684, 723-724`.
- Mailbox ephemeral / recipient binding: fresh ephemeral filled per post (`:413`); `seal_mailbox_payload` signs over `[domain, v, recipientEncPk, senderIdentityPk, payload]` (`mailbox.rs:64-72`) — recipient-, version-, sender-bound; cross-recipient relay-lift is rejected (`relay_to_other_recipient_fails_identity_check`). Sealed pointer binds the scope name via the signed payload; commitment need not be carried (verified at resolve by the adoption gate). Sound.
- Contributory ECDH: `diffie_hellman(...).ok_or(UnusableRecipientKey)` (`:285-288`) and `X25519Public` low-order rejection on construction. Correct.
- Fail-closed convergence gate: `sweep_pass` failure → `Converge`; any `dropped_lost_race` → `SubtreeNotConverged` with nothing minted/published/posted (`:251-265`, tests `non_converged_subtree_is_rejected_fail_closed`, `unresolvable_subtree_is_rejected_fail_closed`).
- Encode-side fail-closed symmetry / release-active tests: no `debug_assert!`/`assert!`/`unwrap`/`panic` in non-test code (grep clean, lines 1-437). Encode-side hard rejects live in `reseal_scope_root` (ledger-diverges, signer-not-committed, dup-tag write body) and are covered by release-active tests in `reseal.rs` (`diverging_ledger_fails_closed_release_active`, `signer_not_committed_fails_closed_release_active`). This slice adds no new encode-side invariant. Determinism test (`creation_is_deterministic_under_a_fixed_entropy_seed`) is not `debug_assertions`-gated, so it fires in release.
- No sensitive logging: no `log`/`tracing`/`println`/`dbg` anywhere in the module; error `check()` strings are key-material-free.
- Register-first / dest-first ordering: grantee published before parent; descendants carried into the new child index before removal from the parent (`:356-403`), verified by `converged_subtree_mints_publishes_and_posts_the_share_pointer`.

Top priority: fix Finding 1 (idempotency key) before merge — it is a live violation of the API's documented mailbox privacy invariant. Then confirm the disposition of Finding 2 (descent re-key) with the owning slice.</result>

## Summary

**Branch:** `feat/engine-cold-seed-seam` · **PR:** #774 (draft) · **Head SHA:** `c6bbd101a098c7fe0215f23732b4c5f025151d94`

**Part 1 (#765.1) — one obvious cold-seed seam.** Removed the redundant unchecked `pointer::cold_seed_floors` wrapper plus its `lib.rs` and `sync/mod.rs` re-exports. Left the natural two layers: the monotonic-max primitive `floor::cold_seed` and the single fail-closed seam `floor::cold_seed_checked` that production `cold_start` uses. The cold-start integration test (`tests/sync.rs`) now drives that same checked seam. Also pruned pointer.rs's now-unused `floor`/`FloorStore` imports and updated its module doc.

**Part 2 (#763) — root-anchor precondition unrepresentable.** New seam shape: `cold_seed_checked(floors, repoint, role: AnchorRole)` where `AnchorRole { Root, Shared }` is the enforcement mechanism. The read-epoch regression check lives **only behind the `Root` arm**; `AnchorRole::Shared` runs solely the unconditionally-sound write-epoch check — so "read-epoch check on a shared scope" cannot be expressed. `cold_start` passes `AnchorRole::Root` (behavior unchanged). Added two tests: shared-scope cold-seed with `minReadEpoch` far below the durable read floor seeds cleanly while the identical input at `Root` fails closed (`FloorRegression::ReadEpoch`); and the write-epoch check still fails closed under `Shared`.

**Part 3 (#765.2) — login_secret probe shape.** Evaluated and kept the raw borrow (the softest branch). The walk needs two *distinct* frozen KDF edges keyed on the raw secret (`owner-pointer-seed` + the unbounded per-index `vault-pointer-index` signer) with no shared intermediate seed; a capability wrapper is pure indirection and a new intermediate edge would ripple into core's KDF/KAT regime. Added a short doc note on `resolve_vault_pointer` (secret already `Zeroizing` at its terminal owner, read-only, single consumer).

**Local gates (from the worktree):** `cargo fmt --all` clean · `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean · `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-core` green · `--release` pass of floor/cold-start tests green (fail-closed checks are release-active `Err` returns, no `debug_assert`, confirmed firing).

**Residual:** none deferred — all three parts landed. Scope stayed confined to the six cold-seed files; no touch to `grants/`, facade.rs, or the decode_queue/replay/dead-letter path. No review skills run (yours to run). PR body carries unescaped `Closes #763`/`Closes #765`, no AI attribution.</result>

## Direct answers to your specific asks

- **Is `AnchorRole` the minimal mechanism, or is there a simpler shape?** The enum is the right shape — do not replace it with a `bool check_read_epoch`, which would be less self-documenting at the call site and wouldn't make the unsound "read-epoch on shared" combination unrepresentable. The derives (`Copy, PartialEq, Eq`) are all used (`role == AnchorRole::Root`) and minimal. No over-parameterization. The only structural question is #3 (variant ahead of consumer), not the enum-vs-alternative choice.
- **Are all removed re-exports cleaned?** Yes, fully. `cold_seed_floors` is gone from both `lib.rs:88` and `sync/mod.rs:49` re-export lists, the `floor`/`FloorStore` imports in `pointer.rs` are trimmed to what remains used, and there is no dangling `pub use` anywhere (grep for `cold_seed_floors` across the branch returns nothing).

<result>I have enough to complete the review. Let me record what I verified.

Verified against the branch:
- `decode_queue` (rebase.rs:123) dead-letters only on `Op::decode` Err; decodable-but-semantically-invalid ops are not reachable here because `cold_start` uses `apply_overlay`, not `replay` (boot.rs:162/203) — so the diff's comment claim holds and there is no misclassification path.
- `StagingStore::remove_op` (seams/staging_store.rs:34) is documented idempotent and targets a single op by strictly-increasing, never-reused `OpId`; staged upload bytes are a fully separate plane keyed by staging keys (`staged` map) and are never touched. Removal correctness and cross-plane separation confirmed — no op_id confusion, no staged-bytes loss.
- Events are an in-process `mpsc::UnboundedSender` (facade.rs:385) — `unbounded_send` enqueues synchronously and does not wait for host observation/persistence.

result: Reviewed PR #773 (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs` cold-start dead-letter drop) — no High/Critical issues; the drop-after-surface behavior matches the documented dead-letter contract and is content-safe. Ranked findings below, all Low/Informational.

---

## Ranked findings

**1. LOW — Non-durable event gate on a durable delete (notification can be lost on crash)** — facade.rs ~525-533
The sequence is `unbounded_send(Event::DeadLetter)` then `remove_op(op_id).await`. The event channel is a best-effort in-process buffer; the send does not wait for the host to drain or persist it. Failure scenario: process crashes after `remove_op` commits but before the host durably observes the buffered `DeadLetter` → the op is permanently gone from the durable queue and the notification vanishes. The user is never told a pending op was discarded.
- Mitigating: only the *notification* is lost — the corrupt op's staged upload bytes live in a separate plane and are retained. No file-content loss.
- Ordering is otherwise the right choice: emit-then-remove means a crash *before* `remove_op` re-decodes and re-emits on next boot (idempotent, since `OpId` is stable/never-reused and `remove_op` is idempotent). Only the crash-after-remove window is unrecoverable, and that's inherent to gating a durable mutation on a non-durable channel.
- Fix direction (optional, only if dead-letter delivery must be guaranteed): this design has no host ack channel, so a true fix isn't cheap; at minimum document that `DeadLetter` is best-effort and hosts must dedup by `op_id`. Otherwise accept as-is.

**2. LOW — `let _ =` swallows send failure but removal proceeds unconditionally** — facade.rs ~527
If the receiver has been dropped (engine tearing down mid-boot), `unbounded_send` returns `Err`, which is discarded, yet `remove_op` still durably purges the entry. The undecodable op is dropped with no surfacing at all.
- Same content-safe caveat as #1 (staged bytes retained).
- Fix direction: gate the removal on a successful surface — `if emit_ok { remove_op(...) }` — so an unsurfaced dead-letter is preserved for the next boot rather than silently purged. Cheap and strictly safer; the only cost is a corrupt entry lingering one extra boot in an abnormal-teardown case.

**3. INFORMATIONAL — Removal is not transactional with boot success** — facade.rs ~525-533
The `remove_op` calls are durable and run before the resolve → floor-seed → gate chain. If that chain later fails-closed (e.g., floor regression halt), boot returns `Err` but the undecodable ops are already permanently purged. Not a security issue (they were undecodable regardless), just noting the purge survives a failed boot. No action needed.

**4. INFORMATIONAL — O(N²) removal shape on a queue full of undecodable entries** — facade.rs loop + host `remove_op`
Bounded work overall (N ≤ staging budget, one `send`+`remove_op` each, no retry loop — DoS-safe), but each `remove_op` is O(N) (`retain` scan in the fake; likely similar in the real journal/IndexedDB host), so a fully-corrupt queue is O(N²) on boot. N is small under the budget, so this is cosmetic. If ever a concern, add a batch `remove_ops(&amp;[OpId])` to the seam.

**5. INFORMATIONAL — Forward-version ops are permanently dropped, by contract** — decode_queue
An op record written by a newer client and opened by an older one is "undecodable" and gets dead-lettered/purged here — a real pending operation is lost in a mixed-version downgrade. This is the documented `#33 D6 / #768` contract (staged bytes are preserved, op intent is not), not a defect of this diff. Flagging only so it's a conscious accepted risk.

---

## What's clean
- **Fail-closed vs. dead-letter**: dropping undecodable entries is the intended behavior, not a fail-closed violation — undecodable is corruption/forward-version, real content (staged bytes) is preserved, and the halt-closed paths (floor regression, adoption gate) are unaffected. No silent data loss of decodable ops.
- **Idempotent crash-resume**: emit-then-remove + never-reused `OpId` + idempotent `remove_op` make re-boot re-emit/re-remove safe (the tests cover the second-boot no-re-emit case).
- **Removal targeting**: single `op_id`, separate staged-bytes plane untouched — no confusion with live decodable ops or upload bytes.
- **Determinism**: no clock/RNG; emission follows `queued_ops` FIFO order; `DeadLetter` deterministically precedes `SnapshotUpdated` (asserted in the added test).
- **Bounded work**: no unbounded/retry loop.

Findings 1 and 2 are the only ones I'd consider folding in before merge (both cheap, #2 the more clearly worthwhile); the rest are accept-as-is.</result>

**Three changes (scoped to `crates/engine/src/facade.rs` only):**

1. **Gated durable removal** — the undecodable loop now runs `remove_op` only on a successful send. New expression:
   ```rust
   for (op_id, _reason) in &amp;undecodable {
       if self
           .events
           .unbounded_send(Event::DeadLetter { op_id: *op_id })
           .is_ok()
       {
           self.seams
               .staging_store
               .remove_op(*op_id)
               .await
               .map_err(ColdStartError::Seam)?;
       }
   }
   ```
   FIFO order and emit-then-remove ordering on the success path preserved.

result: Implemented issue #667 — scheduled dormant-mailbox TTL sweep — on branch `feat/api-mailbox-dormant-sweep`, opened DRAFT PR #777 (`Closes #667`), all API checks green.

## Summary

**Branch:** `feat/api-mailbox-dormant-sweep`
**PR:** #777 (draft, base `main`, labels `v2-build` + `comp:api`)
**Head SHA:** `0d25e7bbc2469a74db1b8821bb15a57ff5f6e0f9`

### What I built
- **Sweep** (`MailboxService.sweepExpired`, `apps/api/src/mailbox/services/mailbox.service.ts`): one injected-`Clock` cutoff snapshot per run; batched `ctid`-bounded set-based deletes (`MAILBOX_SWEEP_BATCH_SIZE`, default 1000) looping until drained, capped per run (`MAILBOX_SWEEP_MAX_BATCHES`, default 1000). Never row-by-row, no long lock, no bloated txn.
- **Cadence:** `MailboxSweepTask` — ~12h `PeriodicTask` (`MAILBOX_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS`), 1h per-run bound, default-on via `MAILBOX_SWEEP_ENABLED`.
- **Single loop / single-flight:** new `common/SchedulerModule` owns one shared `WorkerScheduler`; republisher + mailbox both register their task in `onModuleInit` (runs before the single `onApplicationBootstrap` `start()`), so the scheduler's register-before-start contract stays untouched — no scheduler-contract change, no test rewrite. Single-flight uses the scheduler's own in-flight guard (the republisher's mechanism); batched deletes are idempotent so cross-instance overlap is harmless. Rewired `republisher.module.ts` off its own scheduler.
- **Index decision:** ADDED `idx_mailbox_received_at` (migration `1784692000000` + entity `@Index`). The existing `idx_mailbox_recipient_received` leads with the recipient and can't drive a recipient-less range scan; without a dedicated index each batch would seq-scan a mostly-fresh table.

### Concurrency (specced upfront)
A concurrent post writes `received_at = clock.now()` (≥ cutoff) that `WHERE received_at &lt; cutoff` can never match. Proven by an adversarial real-PG interleave test: a `post` racing a sweep over a 300-row dormant backlog — the fresh post survives, all expired rows deleted.

### Test results (all ran, all green)
- typecheck + build clean; eslint clean
- **153 unit tests** pass (incl. `mailbox-sweep.task.test.ts`, `scheduler.module.test.ts` proving shared-instance + lifecycle ordering)
- **127 real-Postgres integration tests** pass, including the **5 new** `mailbox.sweep.integration.test.ts` cases (ran against the local `cipherbox-postgres` container)
- **Migration-drift check** replicated locally on a throwaway DB → "No changes in database schema were found"

### Residual / notes
- Zero-knowledge preserved: the sweep touches only `ctid`/`received_at`, returns a count, never reads/logs blob bytes.
- Minor dedup: extracted shared `common/config-int.ts` (`positiveIntConfig`) and `common/env-flag.ts` (`isDisabled`), the latter re-exported from `republisher.module.ts` so its existing test import still resolves.
- Review trio (`/simplify`, `/security-review`, `/crypto-privacy-review`) left for you to run as requested; PR left as draft, not flipped to ready.</result>

### Ranked findings

| # | File:line | Finding | Why safe to cut | ~LOC |
|---|-----------|---------|-----------------|------|
| 1 | `republisher/republisher.module.ts:20-22` | `isDisabled` re-export shim kept **only** so `republisher.module.test.ts:2` can keep importing from `./republisher.module`. You have both `export { isDisabled } from '../common/env-flag'` **and** a separate `import { isDisabled } from '../common/env-flag'` for the same symbol — dead compatibility indirection. | Move the parse test to a new `common/env-flag.test.ts` (colocate with impl) and point the test import at `../common/env-flag`. Then delete the re-export line; the module keeps only its own `import`. Nothing else imports `isDisabled` from this module. | ~3 (+1 test import) |
| 2 | `mailbox/services/mailbox.service.ts:44-45,272-273,285-288` + `mailbox-sweep.task.test.ts:487-506` | `MAILBOX_SWEEP_MAX_BATCHES` as a **config knob** is over-parameterization. Default 1000 × batch 1000 = ~1M rows/run — effectively never hit; its real job is "don't loop forever," a role a hardcoded constant fills. The run is *already* bounded twice more: the loop breaks on `deleted &lt; batchSize`, and the scheduler abandons a run past `runTimeoutMs`. | Demote to an internal `SWEEP_MAX_BATCHES` const. Keep the guard, drop the env plumbing + its `positiveIntConfig` call. (One integration test asserts the cap via env; rewrite it to set the const-sized backlog, or keep `BATCH_SIZE` env only.) | ~5 |
| 3 | `mailbox/tasks/mailbox-sweep.task.ts:7-8,28-35` | `MAILBOX_SWEEP_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS` knob is speculative. The `PeriodicTask.runTimeoutMs` seam already has a scheduler-level default (`DEFAULT_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS = 1h`), and this per-task override duplicates it. No caller needs to tune it per-deployment yet. | Drop the env override; let the task expose a fixed `runTimeoutMs` const (or omit it and inherit the scheduler default). Removes one knob + its `positiveIntConfig` call + one test case. | ~4 |
| 4 | `mailbox/mailbox.module.ts:47-53` vs `republisher.module.ts:57-63` | Duplicated opt-out prologue: both `onModuleInit` bodies are the same `if (isDisabled(config.get('&lt;X&gt;_ENABLED'))) return; scheduler.register(task)` shape. | **Borderline — likely leave as-is.** Factoring into a shared helper (`registerUnlessDisabled(scheduler, task, config, envKey)`) would trade 4 trivial lines for a new abstraction + indirection. Two copies is under the rule-of-three; flag only, don't cut. | 0 (note) |

### Explicitly *not* findings (checked, judged sound)

- **`SchedulerModule` extraction + republisher rewiring** — warranted, not indirection. There are now genuinely two `PeriodicTask`s (republisher walk + mailbox sweep) that must share one loop; the old main comment explicitly anticipated exactly this ("one shared loop means exporting this provider or hoisting it"). Moving `start()`/`stop()` into the shared module and switching registration to `onModuleInit` is *required* by the seam's register-before-start contract (Nest runs all `onModuleInit` before any `onApplicationBootstrap`), not gratuitous surface. Keep it.
- **`config-int.ts` extraction** — genuine dedup: `positiveIntConfig` now has 4 call sites across `mailbox.service.ts` (pending cap, poll limit, sweep batch) and `mailbox-sweep.task.ts`. Was already a local fn; promoting it is correct.
- **`env-flag.ts` extraction** — genuine dedup: two consumers (mailbox + republisher). The *extraction* is right; only the re-export shim (finding #1) is the smell.
- **`MAILBOX_SWEEP_BATCH_SIZE` + `MAILBOX_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS` + `MAILBOX_SWEEP_ENABLED`** — earn their place: batch size is a real lock/DoS control, `_ENABLED` mirrors `REPUBLISHER_ENABLED` for out-of-process/single-instance pinning, and `_INTERVAL_MS` matches the republisher cadence-override pattern. All three are exercised by tests.
- **`idx_mailbox_received_at` migration + `ctid`-batched delete** — the recipient-led composite index can't drive a recipient-less scan, so the new index is necessary; `ctid IN (SELECT … LIMIT)` is the standard Postgres `DELETE … LIMIT` workaround. Correct.

If tight: it is tight. Net cuttable surface is roughly **12 LOC across findings 1–3**, and only #1 is unambiguous — #2 and #3 are judgment calls about whether those two knobs should be config or constants (I'd cut both; they overlap with existing bounds). Everything structural in the PR is justified.

result: PR #777 reviewed — clean apart from one dead `isDisabled` re-export shim (republisher.module.ts:20-22, ~3 LOC, unambiguous cut) and two over-parameterized sweep knobs (`MAILBOX_SWEEP_MAX_BATCHES`, `_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS`) that duplicate existing bounds and could be constants; the `SchedulerModule`, `config-int`, and `env-flag` extractions are all warranted.</result>

## Report

### 1. Finding validity — CONFIRMED
`positiveIntConfig` (`apps/api/src/common/config-int.ts`) had no upper bound, so `'2147483648'` (2^31) was accepted verbatim. Both mailbox-sweep and republisher `intervalMs`/`runTimeoutMs` values flow into `setInterval`/`setTimeout` in `TimerWorkerScheduler` (`worker-scheduler.ts`), where Node silently coerces any delay &gt; 2147483647 ms to 1 ms → near-continuous execution. Valid Major/Stability finding.

### 2. Files changed &amp; sites bounded
- **`apps/api/src/common/worker-scheduler.ts`** — added exported constant `MAX_TIMER_DELAY_MS = 2_147_483_647` (placed in the module that owns the timers).
- **`apps/api/src/common/config-int.ts`** — `positiveIntConfig(raw, fallback, max?)`; a value above `max` now fails closed to `fallback`, same as non-positive/non-integer. Signature is backward-compatible (new param optional).
- **`apps/api/src/mailbox/tasks/mailbox-sweep.task.ts`** — bounded `intervalMs` with `MAX_TIMER_DELAY_MS`. **Skipped runTimeoutMs** — the two upstream commits I rebased over (`eb0ce48eb`, `23f31d739`) removed `runTimeoutMs` from this task entirely (it now inherits the scheduler default), so there is no second timer site here.
- **`apps/api/src/republisher/republisher.task.ts`** — bounded `intervalMs` and `runTimeoutMs`. **Skipped** `staleAlertMs` (used only in `Date` arithmetic, not a timer) and `concurrency` (used in `Math.min`, not a timer). Upstream had also replaced this file's local `positiveInt` with the shared `positiveIntConfig`, so the bounding now goes through the shared parser.
- **`apps/api/src/mailbox/tasks/mailbox-sweep.task.test.ts`** — added an overflow case asserting `'2147483648'` for `MAILBOX_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS` falls back to the 12h default.

Bounded: mailbox `intervalMs`; republisher `intervalMs` + `runTimeoutMs`. Skipped with reason: mailbox `runTimeoutMs` (removed upstream), republisher `staleAlertMs`/`concurrency` (non-timer values).

### 3. Commit SHA
`338b1160c8b240ec13cf8dc3f47171b10ca0eab8`, pushed to `feat/api-mailbox-dormant-sweep` (fast-forward `23f31d739..338b1160c`).

## SIMPLIFY

**Overall: essentially clean.** The diff is minimal and every change is load-bearing. The test-helper refactor (`commitment` → `commitment_for` + thin `commitment` wrapper) is *required*, not gold-plating: the old helper hard-coded `ipns_name: b"scope-root"`, which mismatches every test's `current_root = old_name_of(&amp;SCOPE)`, so the new guard would fail all existing tests unless the helper names the real scope root. The new `CommitmentScopeMismatch` variant, its `Display`/`check`/`is_retryable` arms, and the negative test are all necessary and correctly wired.

**Optional (minor) — repeated rationale / mild WHAT-narration**
- Severity: low · `crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs:449-454` (inline `1a)` comment)
- Defect: The "same binding the adoption gate enforces (`gate/adoption.rs`)" rationale is now stated **three times** — module header (54-56), the `CommitmentScopeMismatch` variant doc (262-265), and this inline block — and the inline block's first sentence ("Bind the owner-auth token… MUST name this scope's current root") largely narrates what the next line plainly does. Repo rules call for stating domain rationale once at its home (the variant definition).
- Scenario: reviewers (CodeRabbit/Greptile/`/simplify`) flag the duplicated cross-reference; the inline block could collapse to a one-line pointer.
- **Optional/defer** — not a correctness issue.

The module-header note itself (54-56) is genuine non-obvious rationale (the scope-binding cross-ref to the adoption gate), not spec-restatement — that part is fine to keep.

## SECURITY

**Clean — no must-fix findings.** Verified against every focus point:

- **Fail-closed / release-active:** Returns `Err(WriteRotateError::CommitmentScopeMismatch)`, not `debug_assert!`/`assert!`. Survives release builds. ✓
- **Check ordering:** Placed at step `1a)`, immediately after owner-sig verification (`verify_grant_set`, step 1) and strictly **before** the epoch bump (step 2), the entropy seed mint (step 3), and the entire publish wave (steps 4-7). No seed is minted and nothing is published before the guard. ✓ (`rotate_write.rs:455`)
- **Byte-comparison correctness &amp; parity:** `plan.commitment.ipns_name != plan.current_root_name.as_str().as_bytes()` is byte-for-byte identical to the adoption gate's accept condition at `crates/engine/src/gate/adoption.rs:433` (`section.commitment.ipns_name != candidate.name.as_str().as_bytes()`) — same canonicalization, so a commitment the gate accepted for this root cannot be falsely rejected here, and no false-accept path exists. IPNS names are public identifiers, so the plain `!=` (matching the gate, which reserves `ct_eq` for secret key material) is correct — no constant-time requirement. ✓
- **Non-retryable classification:** Added to the `is_retryable` → `false` arm alongside `NotOwner`/`EpochExhausted`. A scope-binding trust violation is correctly non-retryable (not an availability stall); the test asserts this. ✓
- **Bypass resistance:** The check is unconditional on the sole rotation entry path. The owner signature already covers `commitment` (including `ipns_name`) in its preimage, so an attacker cannot forge a commitment whose `ipns_name` matches a scope they don't own — the two checks compose to bind a valid owner signature to the exact scope under rotation. ✓
- **DoS / determinism / injection:** Pure length-bounded byte compare, no allocation of attacker-controlled size, no clock/RNG — deterministic. ✓

Negative test `commitment_naming_a_different_scope_is_rejected_fail_closed` correctly asserts the `check()` string, non-retryability, **and** empty publish state — proving no side effect leaks before the reject. Positive coverage is implicit: every pre-existing success test now routes through the guard with a matching commitment.

**Net:** Ship-ready. Only the optional triple-stated-rationale comment trim is worth considering before requesting external review, and it is non-blocking.</result>

## Summary

One unresolved thread (Greptile P2), verdict fixed.

**Thread — Greptile P2: `DescendantResolve`/`DescendantMint` partial-commit docs omit earlier siblings** — VALID, FIXED
- File: `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs`, error variant at line ~183 (`DescendantResolve`).
- Root cause confirmed against current code: step 5b (lines 406-464) re-keys each direct-child descendant sequentially — resolve → mint → publish, one descendant at a time. So a `DescendantResolve` or `DescendantMint` failure on descendant N leaves siblings 0..N-1 already re-keyed and published on the network. The doc comment only said "the descendant keeps its old parent derivation," understating the committed surface — exactly what `DescendantPublish` already documents ("any earlier-re-keyed descendants are committed").
- Fix: updated `DescendantResolve` doc to "the grantee root and any earlier-re-keyed descendants are committed; this one keeps its old parent derivation." `DescendantMint` cross-references `DescendantResolve` ("same partial-commit surface as `DescendantResolve`"), so it stays accurate without a separate edit.
- Docs-only change, scoped strictly to `create.rs`. No touch to `rotation/*`.
- Replied (comment 3635115641) and resolved thread `PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6THRTe`.

**Validation:** `cargo build -p cipherbox-engine` clean; `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean. Skipped the full test run — behavior unchanged (comment-only edit).

## Report

Completed both deferred items on PR #783 (`feat/engine-net-envelope-size-cap`). Two commits, pushed. PR left as draft (not flipped, not merged).

### Item A — #788: encode-side symmetry (AGENTS.md rule 8)
- **Shared constant home:** moved `MAX_RESOLVED_RECORD_BYTES` (4 MiB) out of `read.rs` into a new `crates/engine/src/content/limits.rs` (`pub(crate)`, private `mod limits`). Both `read::read_block` (decode side) and `dag::assemble` (encode side) now import the one definition. Its doc comment is updated to state the encode guard now enforces the ~108 GiB flat-DAG ceiling (resolving the old "#788, see" note).
- **Encode guard:** `crates/engine/src/content/dag.rs::assemble` now, after `encode(&amp;root)`, returns a **release-active** `Err(DagError::RootTooLarge { size, limit })` when `root_block.len() &gt; MAX_RESOLVED_RECORD_BYTES` — before `compute_cid`, so an unreadable root is never produced or addressed. New `RootTooLarge { size, limit }` variant added to `DagError`.
- **Release test:** `assemble_fails_closed_when_the_root_exceeds_the_block_cap` builds ~120k dummy 36-byte-CID leaves directly (no file bytes) to push the manifest over 4 MiB and asserts `RootTooLarge`; `assemble_accepts_a_root_just_under_the_block_cap` (~100k links) asserts an under-cap root still assembles Ok and content-addresses. **Confirmed firing in `--release`** (332 lib tests pass in release).

### Item B — #787: peak-memory bound at the fetch boundary
- **Seam contract** (`crates/engine/src/seams/http.rs`): added `Http::send_capped(request, max_bytes) -&gt; Result&lt;HttpResponse, CappedFetchError&gt;` and the `CappedFetchError { Transport(SeamError), BodyTooLarge { observed, limit } }` enum (+ a `CONTENT_LENGTH` const), re-exported from `seams/mod.rs`. The trait default is a buffering backstop (calls `send`, checks `.len()`) — documented as bounding nothing; real content transports override it.
- **read_block** (`content/read.rs`): `fetch` now calls `send_capped(request, MAX_RESOLVED_RECORD_BYTES)`. A `Transport` error rotates to the next source (availability); a `BodyTooLarge` maps to **terminal** `ReadError::TooLarge` (no rotation) — matching the already-shipped over-cap behavior. The post-fetch `.len()` check is **kept as defense-in-depth**.
- **Desktop (`crates/desktop-seams/src/http.rs`, reqwest) — TRUE streaming bound:** refactored the shared prelude into `dispatch()`; `send_capped` does a `Content-Length` pre-check (reject before reading a byte) then a `response.chunk()` streaming loop that aborts once the running total would exceed `max_bytes`. A missing/lying Content-Length is still bounded by the streaming cap. This is the real peak-memory bound.
- **WASM (`crates/wasm/src/seams_bridge.rs`) — Content-Length + buffered fallback, documented residual:** the JS `HttpSeam.send` materializes the whole body before returning to Rust, so `send_capped` there does a `Content-Length` header pre-check plus a body-length check (both fail closed with `BodyTooLarge`). **Residual (flagged in code + here):** a true streaming bound on WASM must live in the JS `HttpSeam` (enforcing `maxBytes` while reading `Response.body`). I did not touch `packages/client` TS (out of the stated scope). CI's WASM leg is authoritative.
- **Tests/mocks:** added `send_capped_rejects_an_over_cap_body_and_admits_one_at_the_cap` to the `ScriptedHttp` fake (`testkit/fakes/http.rs`). No mock/fake signatures needed changing — the `ScriptedHttp`, contract `ReqwestHttp`, and `GatedHttp` doubles all inherit the default `send_capped` and compiled unchanged.

### Build/test results (all local, all green)
- `cargo build/test -p cipherbox-engine` (debug): pass. `--release --lib`: 332 pass, incl. the rule-8 encode test.
- `cargo build -p cipherbox-desktop-seams`: ok. `cargo build -p cipherbox-contract -p cipherbox-fuse`: ok.
- WASM: `cargo build -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`: ok (target was installed). `cargo clippy` wasm target: clean.
- `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine -p cipherbox-desktop-seams --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean.
- Could not run the JS-side WASM behavior (no packages/client change made); the WASM Rust bridge compiles for wasm32.

### PR
- Body patched via `gh api PATCH` — now contains `Closes #787` and `Closes #788` (both unescaped). PR remains **draft / open**.
- New head SHA: **2b1e1f9afa21c1c61b5816977eb451844bca84a2**

### For a security/crypto reviewer
- **Encode/decode symmetry (rule 8):** `assemble`'s `RootTooLarge` is a plain release-active `if` (not `debug_assert`), matching the `body.rs` convention; verify it truly cannot be stripped.
- **Terminal vs rotate semantics:** a `BodyTooLarge` from any source (accelerator or fallback) is terminal — a malicious accelerator serving an oversized body denies the honest fallback. This is **not a regression** (the pre-existing post-fetch `TooLarge` was already terminal and non-rotating), but it's the property to scrutinize.
- **WASM residual:** the WASM path does not yet bound peak memory (JS buffers first); the real fix is a JS `HttpSeam` change, tracked in-code.
- Desktop `observed = body.len() + chunk.len()` cannot overflow usize on 64-bit (loop invariant keeps `body.len() &lt;= max_bytes`).</result>

## Report

### 1. What I implemented (all in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`)

**Read surface.** Added a `snapshot: Snapshot` field to `Engine`, seeded `Snapshot::new(NodeId([0u8;16]))` (the anchored root; cold-start/resolve will replace it — I did not touch those paths). Added public async `Engine::view() -&gt; Result&lt;EngineView, EngineError&gt;` that renders `apply_overlay(&amp;self.snapshot, &amp;pending_ops)`. `EngineView` exposes FUSE-shaped reads: `children(parent)`, `lookup(parent, name)` (strict-comparator name fold via `collation_key`), `attrs(node)`, `statfs()`, plus `root()`. Reads project off the rendered `Snapshot` into a facade-owned `NodeAttrs` (id/name/kind/content_version) so the internal `NodeMeta` doesn't leak. Pending ops come from `staging_store.queued_ops()` decoded via `decode_queue` (same path cold-start uses); undecodable entries are dropped from the render (cold-start owns their dead-lettering). Also added `Engine::root()`.

**Command execution.** Replaced the blanket `Unimplemented` in `command()` with real arms for `Create` (metadata-only), `Delete`, `Rename`, `Relink`. Each builds the `Op` carrying `base_sequence` read from the rendered view's `record_sequence`, calls `stage_op(&amp;staging_store, &amp;profile, &amp;op, None)`, then emits. Create mints a fresh id16 from the injected entropy seam. A **content-bearing** `Create` returns `Unimplemented{"create"}` (content plane is a later slice); `UpdateContent` and all grant/rotation/auth commands stay `Unimplemented`.

**Event emission.** New `stage_and_notify` emits `Event::SnapshotUpdated` (best-effort) on a `Queued` stage outcome.

Supporting: two new `EngineError` variants — `Seam{message}` and `Entropy{message}` (both fail-closed, release-active `Err`, no asserts) — with `Display` arms and a `from_seam` helper. Removed the now-redundant `#[allow(dead_code)]` on the `entropy`/`events` fields and refreshed the module header.

### 2. statfs + pre-start
- **statfs is a deliberate stub**: `StatFs{ nodes }` counts nodes reachable from the root via a cycle-guarded BFS over existing `Snapshot` accessors (no edit to `model.rs`). Quota/byte accounting lives on the API client — not wired here, and the doc says so.
- **Pre-start**: both `command()` and `view()` check `self.started` and return `EngineError::NotStarted` (no panic).

### 3. PR / branch / SHA
- PR **#789** (draft): https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/789 — `Closes #748`, `Part of #635`, labels `v2-build` + `comp:engine` (added via REST; `gh pr edit` hit the known Projects-classic error).
- Branch `feat/engine-facade-wiring`, head SHA `1e3ce6d549f37ce87acc59595bf438d5c6850634`.

### 4. Validation
- `cargo build -p cipherbox-engine` ✅
- `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` ✅ all green (342 lib + 9 integration + others)
- `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` ✅ clean
- `cargo fmt --all --check` ✅ clean

New tests (inline `mod tests` in facade.rs): `command_before_start_returns_not_started`, `view_before_start_returns_not_started`, `create_is_visible_through_the_read_surface_and_emits`, `content_bearing_create_is_unimplemented_pending_the_content_plane`, `delete_removes_the_node_from_the_view`, `rename_updates_the_name_in_the_view`, `relink_moves_the_node_between_folders_in_the_view`, `statfs_counts_reachable_nodes`, `non_metadata_commands_stay_unimplemented`.

### 5. Left out / follow-ups
- **Had to edit `crates/engine/tests/facade.rs`** (outside `src/facade.rs` but not in your protected-module list): the integration test `every_command_returns_its_typed_unimplemented_error` asserted the old all-`Unimplemented` contract this slice deliberately changes. I retargeted it to `unimplemented_commands_return_their_typed_error` covering only the still-unimplemented surface (content-bearing create, updateContent, grants/rotation/auth). Flagging since it's a test file adjacent to the strict scope.
- **Content plane deferred**: content-bearing `Create` and `UpdateContent` remain `Unimplemented` (sealing/entropy = later slice), as instructed.
- **Base-snapshot projection is a later slice**: until resolve replaces the base snapshot, the base is only the anchored root and all visible children come from the overlay — which is exactly what the tests exercise (create-under-root, etc.). This is a stated limitation, not a bug.
